The end trigger pass uses any nonzero world still stored in the camera record, so an interior-world miss can leave it querying the previous world's trigger grid even though the current entry is null. It is a vertical ground probe, not a line from the eye toward the focus, so it is not the line-of-sight test of Walls and the stuck timers.
End of the update (High). After the probe the update does only two more things: it stores the room index, and, in every mode and when a ground world is stored at 0x1D8, it runs a trigger pass at the camera position. The trigger pass looks up the camera's grid cell in the world of its ground entry and, for each collision primitive there of kind 1 or kind 8, runs a point test with a 1.0 radius at the camera position and hands a hit to the world's trigger handler. It never moves the camera. There is no second orbit or shrink pass in the update; the only shrink is the one in Recentre pass and R3. Test vector: camera at (0, 5, 0) in a cell with one kind 1 and one kind 3 primitive: only the kind 1 primitive is tested, and the camera position is (0, 5, 0) after the pass whatever the result. Last step of the camera tick (High): the ambient-sound reference is fed. In mode 0 with a focus that has a ground entry, it takes the map byte at +0x0C of that entry and the entry's world; in every other case (any other mode, a scripted play, or no focus ground entry) it takes the map byte and world of the camera's own ground entry when it has one, else map 0 and the current world. Then the sound system's emitter tick runs (SPEC-0031).

### Ground clearance (Confidence: High; read word by word from the code, 2026-11-08)

The routine runs every follow tick from the spring step (after the spring has moved the eye, before the wall pass) and from the dormant orbit. Steps, in order:
1. The probe point is the eye's x and z with the start height eye.y + 2.5 (an immediate of the code, not a data word). The probe runs on the current world first, with the camera's ground-entry slot cleared; if the entry is still 0 the same probe runs on the other resident world. There is no null-world test and no interior-buffer exception here.
2. The ground-world word (0x1D8) is written on every call, after the probes and whether anything hit: the other world when the second probe hit, else the current world (first-probe hit, or both missed). The stored ground height (0x1DC) is the float the last probe call returned (the second probe's result when the first missed, even if both missed).
3. Only when the entry is nonzero: the heightfield record of that entry's world that belongs to the entry (the record index is a halfword of the entry) is looked up; when present, its bilinear height sample at (x, z) replaces the local height h used below (when the point is outside the sample grid the probe height is kept). The stored 0x1DC keeps the probe value. So the floor is the bilinear heightfield height under the eye when the hit entry has a heightfield record, else the probe height; the sample overwrites, it is not a maximum with the probe height.
4. floor = h + 2.0 (ELF word 0x2DB3C4). If eye.y >= floor nothing moves.
5. Otherwise let u = the unit vector from the eye to the focus F. The eye slides when all hold: u.y > 0 (the focus is above the eye), the focus character's flag test (the "hero not moving" class test) returns 0, and floor < F.y. Slide: the reseat flag (0x198) = 1; the eye motion direction (0x100) = (0, 0, 0, 1); E' = E + u x ((E.y - floor) / (-u.y)), so E'.y = floor exactly, w = 1; the eye (0xE0) and the previous eye (0xF0) = E'; the polar of (E' - F) goes to the current polar (0x140) and the target polar (0x130), all lanes, and its elevation to the manual elevation (0xC4) and the elevation target (0x154). The spin value, the side-swing counter and the spring velocity are not touched.
6. Otherwise (a slide condition fails) the eye is raised: E.y = floor; x, z and w stay and nothing else is written (no polar, previous eye or motion-direction write).
7. On every path the eye's w is forced to 1.
So the slide keeps the eye on the line to the focus and resets the follow state; the raise only lifts y.
0. A separate minimum-height rule, used by some camera paths, raises the camera position (0x30) to at least the profile minimum height (+0x20, 1.0) above the stored ground height (0x1DC), re-probed at its x, z when the camera has a ground entry (0x1D4). The mode 7 entry applies the same rule with 100.0.

### Walls and the stuck timers (Confidence: High for the flow, the order and the timers (confirmed from the code 2026-11-08); Medium-High for the push geometry)

After the spring step the eye (0xE0) is pushed out of, in this order:1. the world's list of 0xB0-byte object records (count at world +0x580C, records from +0x5810): the moving objects whose active word is set (each record's position quad, the offset-from-home quad, is copied into its source box primitive and the box push runs with the gates skipped: no mask word, no class bits);2. the ground frame records of the grid cell under the eye (entries whose gate word is 0 or shares a bit with the collision word of the camera's ground world; records of type 6, the low byte of +0x4C, are skipped, and both camera wall passes skip them: this per-tick eye pass and the occlusion clip of the pull-in setter, a different routine that is described in Camera on world entry). Each record is tested with the single-margin plane test, whose margin is the eye push radius 1.25 (0x2DB400): a wall hit reports the depth 1.25 - d inside the margin, a ground hit reports zero depth (see Camera collision and occlusion, camera plane test; Medium-High). This is not the characters' test: the heroes use the two-parameter plane test of SPEC-0040 (reach = body radius 1.175 only; the body height 3.5 only sets the vertical window), and they do test type 6 records, as two-sided walls (SPEC-0018, Frame-record type and type-6 survey). So the eye passes through type 6 walls that stop the hero;3. the collision primitives of that cell (cell list A of SPEC-0018), by the kind byte at +0x10 of the loaded primitive: 1 plane; 3 cylinder; 4 or 7 with the radius value +0x2C above 0 box push, 4 or 7 with +0x2C at or below 0 zero-radius contact; 2 (cone) skipped; other kinds ignored. Kind-7 rectangles are not skipped by this dispatch (the door rectangles of pack 1098 beside its arrival locator 1 are kind 7 with flags 0x2, at z -54.65 and z -67.60): they go to the zero-radius or the box route by their radius value, subject to the mask and class gates. Resolved: every door rectangle on disc (all 169 doors; mask 0, flags 0x2) carries only class bit 1, so it fails the class gate of the zero-radius route (class bit 0 or 5) and of the line-of-sight sweep, and neither pushes nor blocks the camera (High; Arrival census by pack). The box push uses an eye radius of 1.25 (0x2DB400);4. the world's near-tree trunk list (foliage patches, not characters; see Objects and the camera). The heroes, the pet, enemies and NPCs are not tested.
This is the pass that SPEC-0018 calls its contact pass (driver): it belongs to the camera, not to the characters.
Each push adds its normal to a contact list (at most 19 entries; a normal within 0.999 of a stored one only refreshes it). Each entry lives 40 ticks (word 0x2DB40C). Confirmed 2026-11-09 (High) by the contact-add routine: a new normal whose dot with a stored entry's normal is above 0.999 sets that entry's lifetime word back to 40; otherwise it is appended (capacity 19, entries of 0x20 bytes at +0x280 with the lifetime at +0x10 of the entry, count at +0x500) with lifetime 40; a full list prints a warning and appends nothing. The same entry layout is the motion record's blocking-normal list (SPEC-0040, Behavior 13). The countdown runs on a tick only while the focus hero is moving (hero state 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 or 32, or sneak state 22, 39 or 40 while the hero's current animation request is 85) or the hold word (0x26C) is nonzero; with neither, entries do not age. The scan visits one slot more than the list holds (SPEC-0018, Follow-camera contact-normal countdowns).
Stuck timer: driven by the eye pass failure flag, not by every push (see Eye pass failure flag below). On a failing tick the counter increases; when two or more contacts are active, or once the restore latch is set, the eye is put back at the previous tick's final eye (0xF0). When the counter exceeds (int)(0.05 x tick rate) = 3 (ELF word 0x2DB3E8 times the run-time tick rate, 60), that is on the fourth consecutive failing tick, the recovery (recentre) pass runs and the state clears. A tick with no failure while the latch is clear resets the counter.
Line-of-sight timer: if the line from the eye to the focus is blocked, a counter increases. The line is blocked when the world segment query from the eye to the focus returns a hit (tried first), or, only if that is clear, the camera sweep test of the same segment returns a hit (from the game code, not run in the original; High for which routine calls what, Medium-High for the per-source gates). **Two different sweep routines exist; do not merge them.** (i) The line-of-sight sweep (the "sweep" of this paragraph, also used for the far-point sight line of the side probes in Eye pass per-primitive routines) is a boolean test of the segment from the eye to the focus, reads the current world only, never moves the camera and has five sources, listed here. (ii) The eye-transient object sweep is a separate aggregate that runs the five per-object occlusion tests of Camera on world entry (Occlusion clip) on the cell under the (possibly already moved) eye: it saves the eye, lets each test move the eye transiently (each sees the eye as moved by the previous ones, with no early exit), and if any test fired writes the saved eye back and returns 1, else 0. It has no movable-block source, it does not skip frame records of type 6, and its mask gates read the camera's ground world (0x1D8). Which callers use (ii) is not established (Open questions); the timers use (i). The sweep (i) checks these sources in this order and stops at the first hit; each is a segment-versus-surface crossing test, and none skips a primitive because it holds an end point: (1) the world's wall groups (a full three-dimensional distance prefilter: a group is admitted when |C - camera position word|^2 / 2 < Q, with no direction used, High; then every face record of an admitted group is tested as a two-sided rectangle crossing with the plane parameter window -0.02 to 1.02 and the face rectangle widened by 0.25 along the run and below, but cut by 0.25 at the top (Occlusion clip, Face test); most likely the type-10 climb and ladder face groups, Medium-Low for that identity); (2) the plate pool: each used entry's cylinder primitive, with the cursor quirk of the clip's step 2 (an entry whose used word +4 is nonzero is not stepped over, so nothing after the first such entry is tested; High); (3) the one grid cell under the camera's position word (the camera body position, last tick's final position, not the cells along the segment; Medium): (a) its frame records of every type except 6, as two-sided rectangle crossings, with no gate word and no mask test (High; no gate word is read anywhere in the query, the sweep or the face test), and (b) its list A primitives by kind (kinds 1, 3, 4 and 7 only; kind 2 and every other kind do nothing; no mask or class bit is read in the sweep routine itself, the gates live in the per-kind tests), under the mask gate of the current world's collision word (a zero mask passes, otherwise it must share a bit with it): kind 1 spheres (class bit 0 or 1 set and class bit 2 clear; the segment against the sphere's horizontal circle, height ignored; this is the sweep's own sphere test, not the three-ray 3D test of the occlusion clip), kind 3 cylinders (the cylinder clear test, read in full 2026-11-09, High: the mask gate against the game's current world, class bit 0 or 4 required and class 5 rejecting, a broad phase that needs the squared horizontal distance between the camera position at +0x30 and the cylinder base strictly below 2500.0 (50 units), then the line-against-finite-cylinder test with a root counting only strictly inside (0, segment length)), kinds 4 and 7 with a positive third extent, the boxes (class bit 3 set, else class bit 0; a ray-versus-ball prefilter with the very large radius 20 times the largest extent, a three-dimensional ball and not an XZ circle (SPEC-0018, Sweep of a moving body against a box), then the five face-rectangle crossing tests of the oriented box, the top face never tested; a hit on any face counts; Medium-High for this sweep variant), kinds 4 and 7 with a third extent of zero or less, the flat rectangles such as doors (class bit 0 or 5 must be set, otherwise ignored; one two-sided rectangle test; Medium); (4) the world's near-tree trunk list (foliage patches, not characters): every plant of every listed patch as a horizontal circle of the trunk radius, tested only when the plant's horizontal squared distance to the eye argument is below 2500.0 (strict; High: the plant base and the eye are the two points compared; the earlier reading as an eye-to-focus distance is withdrawn); the radius and height are read once per patch and applied to every plant. The segment runs from the focus toward the eye, the circle centre is the plant raised by half the height, and the circle test returns the crossing parameters: with one root the line is blocked only when 0 < t < segment length, with two roots when either lies in (0, length). Height is not a bound (it only places the circle centre, which a horizontal circle test ignores); (5) the active movable blocks (active word nonzero), always through the box test (never the flat-rectangle branch): the block's current position quad is first written into the reference point of its source box primitive, then the box test runs with the skip-gates argument 0. (This source (5) exists only in the line-of-sight sweep (i), not in the eye-transient aggregate (ii).) A primitive that holds the eye or the focus is not skipped: a segment lying wholly inside a box crosses no face and is clear, a segment with one end inside crosses a face on its way out and is blocked, and for circle tests one end inside is blocked and both ends inside is clear (Medium: this follows the root-count rule above but the circle helper itself was not re-read). The eye is normally pushed out of boxes (radius 1.25) before this test, so the focus (hero + 3.5 up) is the end that can sit inside one. Class and mask gates matter more than geometry: a kind 4 or 7 primitive blocks the line only if its class bits allow it, so the pack 1098 door rectangles beside locator 1 (kind 7, flags 0x0002, class bit 1 only) never block the camera line; whether that pack's door pillar boxes block depends on their flags, which are not measured (open: the list A primitives in the cell at the arrival camera position (78.39, -124.6, -61.15) and their flags). The world segment query (read first for the timer) lifts both end points by 1.5 in y (which also moves the boxes tested relative to them), is blocked outright when the end heights differ by more than 50.0 (strict), reads only the cells under the two end points (the focus end first, then the eye or candidate end), tests every record of every entry with the face test, and skips records of type 6 when its restriction argument is 1; it reads the current world and no gate word. The counter increases on each blocked tick; when it exceeds (int)(1.0 x tick rate) = 60 (word 0x2DB3EC times the run-time tick rate, 60), that is on the 61st consecutive blocked tick, the recentre pass runs and the counter, the blocked flag and the previous blocked flag are cleared (High). A clear tick resets the counter. The world segment query of this test is made with its restriction argument set to 1, the same setting as the line test of the recentre pass (High for the argument; Medium for what it restricts). The two timers are independent: the stuck timer reads the eye pass failure flag, this one reads the line-of-sight result, and both run in the same pass, the stuck timer first.

### Camera collision and occlusion (Confidence: High for the sources, the worlds and the constants; High for the plane-test windows and the occlusion clip's face test; Medium for moving door walls)

This subsection collects the rules that keep the follow camera from ending up inside or behind a wall or door during normal play. It adds nothing to the passes above; where a number appears it is the same number. Everything here is from the game code, not run in the original.

**Shape and method.** The eye is a sphere of radius 1.25 (0x2DB400). There is no swept sphere and no ray from the target: each tick the spring moves the eye freely, then a push-out pass moves it out of whatever it overlaps (Walls and the stuck timers). A second, independent test is a zero-thickness segment from the eye to the focus (focus = hero position + 3.5 up, plus the small bias); it never moves the eye, it only feeds the 60-tick line-of-sight timer.
**What the push-out pass tests** (the eye pass; mode 0 every tick; modes 8 to 10 use the same pieces in their contact pass):
| Source | Used | Gate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Level frame records in the eye's grid cell (walls, ledges, ground and ceiling slabs) | all types except 6 | the entry gate word passes the collision word of the camera's ground world (a zero gate always passes) |
| Cell list A primitives of the eye's cell (spheres, cylinders, boxes, flat rectangles such as door panels) | by kind, see Eye pass per-primitive routines | primitive mask word against the collision word of the camera's ground world, then the class bits |
| Movable blocks (the 0xB0-byte records) | only those whose active word is nonzero; box push with radius 1.25 | none (no mask, no class bits) |
| Tree trunks: the plants of the foliage patches in the world's near-tree list (not characters) | all listed | none |
| Placed-object inline volumes (the pushes that stop characters) and the plate pool | not tested | - |
The camera's ground world is the world that supplied the camera's ground entry in the ground clearance probe (below); the same world's lists and collision word are used for all four sources in one tick.

**What the sight-line test reads** (blocked => timer +1; the 61st consecutive blocked tick runs the recentre pass): the segment query of SPEC-0018 on the current world's grid only (frame records of the two endpoint cells, type 6 skipped, no gate word read, blocked outright when the endpoints differ in height by more than 50), then, if that is clear, the line-of-sight sweep (i) of Walls and the stuck timers on the current world only (wall groups with the full 3D prefilter, the plate pool with its cursor quirk, the cell under the camera position word, the near-tree trunks, the active movable blocks) with the primitive mask gate read from the current world's collision word and the class gates listed in Walls and the stuck timers. (High for the world: all three of the query, the sweep and the sight-line routines read the current world, never the camera ground world of 0x1D8; the occlusion clip of the recentre commit is the one that reads 0x1D8.) The eye-transient aggregate (ii) described there is a different routine and is not part of this test.

**Camera plane test (frame records; High for the rules and windows, Medium-High for the start-of-tick position).** For a record with unit normal n and plane offset o, let dP = n . P + o for the eye P (after this tick's spring step) and dB the same for B = P minus the spring velocity (the eye's displacement of the tick; when the eye was just moved B is where it started the tick, and when the move was skipped B is a point one velocity behind P), and let r = 1.25. The edge-height helper returns T = (the height at the tested point's x and z of the record's top plane) + D / m.y - 0.25, with m the top plane's normal and D the record's stored depth; that is H_top - 0.25 where H_top is the true top height of SPEC-0018 (Frame record as an edge: top-plane height plus depth over m.y). High for the formula (read from the code); the one statement used everywhere below is the window T - D - 1.25 < y < T - 0.3, which in true-top terms is H_top - D - 1.5 < y < H_top - 0.55. The test is one-sided and tunnel-safe.
- Wall record (|n.y| at most 0.9): a hit needs dP > 0 or dB > 0 (at least one end in front of the plane), and dP <= r. The position along the record's run axis, for P or for B, must lie in [-r, L + r] with L the run length. The eye's height y (the eye's current height in both tries) must satisfy T - D - r < y < T - 0.3, with T taken at P, and if that fails with T taken at B. In terms of the true top plane: H_top - D - 1.5 < y < H_top - 0.55. The reported depth is r - dP, so a hit always moves the eye to exactly 1.25 in front of the plane, even from behind it when dB > 0. An eye with dP <= 0 and dB <= 0 (wholly behind a one-sided wall, or exactly on it without having come from the front) is not touched.
- Up-facing record (n.y > 0.9, ground): a hit needs dP < 0.05, the run window as above and the height window T - D - r < y < T (at P, else at B); depth is reported as 0, so it counts as a contact and moves the eye by nothing. Down-facing record (ceiling): same with dP < r.
- The run window [-r, L + r] is empty for any L below -2.5 (-2 r). Placed objects store -200 as the run length while they are hidden, so their walls do not exist for the camera.
Test vectors (wall record, normal (0, 0, 1) through z = 0, offset 0, run along x from 0 to 40, top plane at 20 with depth 20, so T = 19.75 and the height window is -1.5 < y < 19.45): the eye moves from z = 1.5 to z = 0.9 at x = 10, y = 5: dB = 1.5, dP = 0.9, hit, depth 0.35, the eye ends at z = 1.25. The eye moves from z = 1.5 to z = -0.5 (velocity (0, 0, -2)): dB = 1.5 > 0, hit, depth 1.75, eye ends at z = 1.25. The eye moves from z = -2 to z = -3: both behind, no hit, the eye stays at z = -3. The eye sits at z = 0 and its velocity is 0 (B = P): dP = dB = 0, no hit; with velocity (0, 0, -0.4) (B at z = 0.4): hit, depth 1.25, eye ends at z = 1.25. The eye behind at z = -2 with velocity (0, 0, -3): B at z = 1.0, dB > 0, hit, depth 3.25, the eye is put at z = 1.25, in front of the wall. Run: x = 41 (run position 41 <= 41.25) hits, x = 45 does not. Height: y = 19.5 gives no hit (19.5 is not below 19.45; the eye passes over the wall), y = 19.4 hits, y = 20.0 no hit, y = 19.0 hits; at the low end y = -1.6 no hit, y = -1.4 hit.

**Eye starting behind a panel or inside a volume (settled; High from the code, not run in the original).** The two passes differ in kind. The occlusion clip of a placement moves the eye along the line to the focus, so an eye behind a face on that line is pulled through the face to the focus side (Occlusion clip). The per-tick pass never uses the focus: it pushes along the record's own normal.
1. One-sided panels. An eye that starts, and whose tick-start position B also is, behind the panel (dP <= 0 and dB <= 0) is ignored: it is neither pushed out to the hero's side nor pushed further through. If B is in front (a front-to-back crossing this tick, or a spring velocity larger than the depth behind the plane that points through it) the eye is put back at exactly 1.25 in front of the plane (depth r - dP, which can be larger than 1.25). If the eye is in front within 1.25 the push is the same. The side the eye is sent to is the side the normal points to, whatever side the hero is on; a panel never pushes an eye that is wholly behind it. At an entry the spring velocity of the old camera is carried over (Fields: reset or kept), so the typical case is a camera at rest and B = P: an eye placed behind a door wall stays there.
2. Volumes. A box (list A kinds 4 and 7 with a positive third extent, and the movable blocks) pushes an eye whose centre lies inside the box grown by 1.25 out through the face the box push chooses (SPEC-0018, Point versus box with radius r, Six faces and Face rules: smallest depth wins, the bottom face's +0.75 bias, the top gate, the corner rule), by that depth plus 1.25. The choice is geometric: it can be the face on the far side from the hero. Test vector (box x 0 to 4, z 0 to 4, y 0 to 6; eye (2, 3, 2.5); depths: top 2.9, bottom 3.75, low z 2.5, high z 1.5, high x 2, low x 2): the high-z face wins and the eye goes to (2, 3, 5.25), whichever side the hero is on. Spheres and cylinders push the eye radially out in the horizontal plane to the radius plus 1.25; flat rectangles (kinds 4 and 7 with a third extent at or below 0) are pushed from along the eye's velocity and need class bit 0 or 5, so door rectangles (class bit 1 only) are ignored.
3. A doorway between two panels. A door rectangle across the gap does nothing in either pass (it carries class bit 1 only), so only the flanking panels, and the door model's own walls while the door is shown, count. In the clip the eye passes whenever its line to the focus crosses the wall plane in the gap, at least 0.25 from each panel end. In the per-tick pass a panel has an effective run [-1.25, L + 1.25], so an eye centre passes a gap of width W only inside the central band of width W - 2.5 (none when W <= 2.5), and only when it is outside both panels' widened runs while it is within 1.25 in front of the plane. Test vector (two panels in the plane z = 0, A run x 0 to 10 and B run x 14 to 24, a gap of 4, top and depth as above): an eye at (12, 5, 0.9) with velocity (0, 0, -0.6) has run position 12 on A (above 10 + 1.25 = 11.25) and -2 on B (below -1.25): no hit; the next move to (12, 5, -2) with velocity (0, 0, -2.9) is not touched either (the run windows fail for B at z = 0.9 too). At x = 11 the same move hits A (11 <= 11.25) and the eye is put at z = 1.25; at x = 12.8 it hits B (run position -1.2 >= -1.25). The free band is 11.25 < x < 12.75 (width 1.5 = 4 - 2.5). Neither pass moves an eye behind the wall line back out through the gap on its own; a blocked line to the focus recentres after 61 ticks.

**No zoom-in rule; how the camera recovers.** There is no rule that shortens the follow distance gradually. The sequence is: (1) the push-out moves the eye out by the contact depth in the same tick (a jump, no speed limit); (2) the spring keeps pulling the eye toward its target distance every tick (critically damped, 0.19 of an offset left after 10 ticks), so the eye slides back out along the wall as soon as nothing overlaps it; (3) if the push cannot be resolved for more than 3 ticks in a row (stuck timer), or the sight line is blocked for 61 ticks, the recentre pass places the eye at once: behind the hero at the target distance, else in front, then shortened by 0.25 per step while the line is blocked, never to 0.75 or below (Recentre pass and R3: a shrink pass runs only while the radius is above 1.0 and then lowers it by a full 0.25, so the last radius lies in (0.75, 1.0], exactly 1.0 when the start is 1.0 plus a multiple of 0.25, and the last shortened candidate is not tested). So the smallest eye-to-focus distance that the recentre pass produces is just above 0.75 (a start of 1.1 or 4.1 ends at 0.85) and the push-out keeps the eye 1.25 from every tested wall; the hero is made see-through below 5 and invisible below 1.7 (Fade ramp). The ground clearance keeps the eye at least 2.0 above the ground under it (Ground clearance); the pull-in setter of a placement halves the horizontal offset when the ground at the wanted eye is more than 5 above the focus (Pose setter with pull-in).

**Hero with his back to a wall or door (derived; Medium-High).** The camera trails behind the hero, so it is normally on the wall side. With the hero's focus at horizontal distance a from an active wall and the eye wanting to be at the target distance on the wall side, the eye is stopped by the push-out at 1.25 from the wall plane. If a < 1.25 the stopping point is on the far side of the hero (distance 1.25 - a past his axis), and the eye then sits almost straight above the focus. Test vector: wall plane z = 0 (open side z > 0), tall; hero feet at z = 1.0, ground y = 0, so the focus is (0, 3.5, 1.0); the eye wants z = 1.0 - 9.55 = -8.55, y = 5.4357 (profile 0 at elevation 0.2). The spring step never moves the eye through in one tick; each tick the eye is pushed back to z = 1.25. Resting eye (0, 5.4357, 1.25): horizontal distance to the focus 0.25, eye-to-focus distance sqrt(0.25^2 + 1.9357^2) = 1.9517; the hero's fade target is 9.76 (nearly clear, reached on tick 24 from opaque). The sight line is clear (the wall is behind both ends), the stuck counter is 0 while the push resolves, so the recentre pass does not run; if the push cannot be resolved (both side probes blocked) it runs on the fourth failing tick. If the wall is only 3.0 high, the eye at y = 5.44 is above its top edge minus 0.3, the wall does not push, and the eye stays at the wanted distance beyond it.

**Door objects (Medium-High for shown and hidden; Medium for moving doors).** A door is a placed object whose walls are frame records of its model set (SPEC-0028). The camera's frame-record test treats the door like any other wall while its run length is 0 or more, which holds from the pose evaluation made by InitObjOn, ActivateObj, SetObjToTime or a play call until DeactivateObj stores -200 in the run length. While the door is hidden (state 3 at load, or after DeactivateObj) the walls neither push the eye nor block the sight line. The camera does not skip animating objects: each pose evaluation rewrites the record from the object's current pose, and the camera tests that record, so a door in motion is a wall at its current position. The records stay listed only in the grid cells covering the door's whole swept footprint (cell membership is static), and an object whose zone query fails is not re-posed, so its walls stay at the last pose. The door's inline type-11 volumes do not exist for the camera (they only push characters). Frame records of type 6 are never tested by any camera test (push-out, sight line, recentre line test); the camera passes through them. Test vector: a door panel with run length 3.0 and normal (1, 0, 0) at x = 10, with the eye inside the panel's run and height windows, moving from x = 11.5 to x = 10.4: hit, depth 1.25 - 0.4 = 0.85, eye ends at x = 11.25. After DeactivateObj the run length is -200: the same move has no hit and the sight line through x = 10 is not blocked. After ActivateObj the walls are live on that call. Round 2 (Objects and the camera, below) adds the state table, the gated walls, the arrival measurement and the correction that no character is tested.

**Two worlds at an exterior and interior door (High for the worlds; Medium-High for the visible result).** The camera's ground world is chosen each follow tick by the ground clearance probe: the current world first, and only if it has no ground at the eye's x and z (probed from 2.5 above the eye), the other resident world; with both missing it is the current world. The follow camera applies no interior-buffer exception here (that exception belongs to the per-tick probe of the non-follow modes). The push-out pass uses that world's cells, collision word and movable blocks. The sight-line test always uses the current world. Consequences: the eye can sit inside the other world's geometry whenever the current world has ground at the eye (the other world's walls are then neither pushed against nor tested for sight); when the eye is over ground that only the other world has, that world's walls push it, but its walls still do not block the sight line. At a door between an exterior and an interior the hero's own world is current, so an eye that has slid across the door line behind the hero is tested against the world it is over, and the sight line only against the hero's world. Test vector: current world W1 has ground at the eye and W2 has a wall 0.5 from the eye: the camera ground world is W1, W2's wall does nothing. W1 has no ground at the eye and W2 does: the push-out uses W2's cell and W2's collision word; a W2 wall between the eye and the focus does not block the sight line.

#### Objects and the camera (Confidence: High for which routines read placed objects and for the hidden and shown rules; Medium-High for the plane-test windows; Medium for the trunk radius and for the arrival measurements, which use static data only)

Round 2 of the door-camera work (from the game code and disc data; not run in the original). It answers what placed objects (type 29 of SPEC-0028), moving platforms, door objects, hidden objects, characters and gated walls do to the follow camera.

**1. What the camera reads of a placed object.** Only the frame records (walls, ledges and slabs) of the object's inline model set, and only through the level grid. The grid lists an object's model-set entries in its cells next to the pack's own entries (10,867 of the 298,734 cell entries on disc name an object's inline model set; SPEC-0018, Grid cells). The eye pass reads the cell under the eye; the sight-line test reads the cells of its two end points and, in its sweep part, the cell under the camera position word; so an object's wall counts only in a cell where its entry is listed. The shape tested is the frame record itself: the one-sided plane with a run window and a height window (Camera plane test above). It is not a bounding box, not a cylinder and not the mesh. Nothing in the camera reads an object's reference centre, radius, drawn byte, state word or colour. Everything else an object owns is invisible to the camera: its inline type-11 volumes (the cylinders, spheres, boxes and rectangles that push characters; they are never in the grid's list A, which holds only the pack's top-level volumes), its height patches (ground only; the ground clearance reads a patch as ground while the object's drawn byte is nonzero), and its mesh. Frame records of type 6 are skipped (17 of the 3,495 objects have such records, 98 records in all). On disc the placed objects' frame records are of type 0 (4,728 records), 3 (2,155), 7 (216), 6 (98) and 5 (4); no placed-object entry has a gate word (the gated walls of item 6 are all level entries). Of the 3,495 objects 642 have frame records and 426 of those move.

**2. In which position.** The record as last written. A pose evaluation (InitObjOn, ActivateObj, SetObjToTime, the offset and rotation setters in states 8, 4 and 10, and each tick of an object in state 0, 2, 5 or 6 whose zone query passes) rewrites the record from the object's current pose; between evaluations the record stays where it was left. So a finished clip keeps its walls at the end pose, and an object whose zone query fails keeps its walls at the last pose whether it is drawn or not. The camera never uses the rest pose of a moving object after the first evaluation and never predicts: a lift or a door in motion is a wall at its current position, tested with the eye's own displacement of the tick as the previous position (the wall's own displacement is not used). Consequences of the one-sided plane test (derived; Medium-High): a wall that moves onto a stationary eye from its open side pushes the eye along with it, each tick to exactly 1.25 in front of the plane, with no speed limit; an eye that is behind the plane both before and after the tick is never touched, so a wall that ends its motion with the eye still behind it leaves the eye inside the geometry; if the plane sweeps across the eye so that the eye's distance in front becomes positive and at most 1.25, the eye is pushed out to the open side. Cell membership is static, so a moved wall outside the cells that list its entry is not tested.

**3. Hidden and shown (Medium-High).** The camera follows the run length of the record and nothing else.
- Never initialised (state 3, 3,491 of the 3,495 objects on disc) or after DeactivateObj: run length -200, an empty run window. No push and no sight-line block, in either world, from that moment.
- InitObjOn, ActivateObj and SetObjToTime evaluate the pose on the call, so the walls are live for the next camera pass. PlayAnim and LoopAnim on a hidden object only flip the state; the first pose, and so the walls, come from the next object step (one tick later).
- A shown object stays a wall while it fades (the colour bytes are draw-only) and with its alpha near 0. An object that is hidden only by the zone query or by the layer filter of its mesh keeps its walls.
- An offset or rotation setter called on a deactivated object (state 4) re-evaluates the pose and restores the walls while the object stays invisible (SPEC-0028, Placed-object states); the camera then meets an invisible wall.
- The sight-line sweep reads the type-10 wall groups (ladder and climb faces) by the same run-length rule, so the three owned groups (pack 777 object 12, pack 800 object 0, pack 1098 object 6) are -200 while their object is hidden. The group's centre and squared radius, used as the distance prefilter, stay at the rest pose.

**4. Door models.** A door that the hero walks through is one of three things: a level-mesh wall of the pack's own model set (the common case), a rectangle trigger plane (no camera effect, above), or a placed object. Placed doors are rare: of the 113 door rectangles in the 117 packs that have placed objects, six lie within 3 units of a placed object's wall: pack 880 door 0 (two sliding leaves, objects 0 and 1, initialised at their start pose by the pack's Init and never played; objects 2 and 3 are a second pair that is never initialised and has no walls; 91 keys, each leaf moves 6.1 units), pack 909 door 0 (object 8, static), pack 1023 door 0 (object 5, a lift that rises 14.08), pack 1139 door 1 (object 0, a sliding panel with 4.32 of travel, at its start or end pose by game state 0x23), pack 1300 door 0 (object 0, 50 static records) and pack 1335 door 0 (object 1, 4 static records). The visible door mesh is never an obstacle by itself. A placed door with live walls pushes the eye and blocks the sight line like any panel; a door mesh with no frame records, or whose walls are off, does nothing, so the eye can sit inside a drawn door mesh. The protection is where the eye is put (Arrival census by pack), the eye pass's push, and the two timers. Whether a placed door mesh without walls sits at a doorway was not measured (the mesh bounds were not decoded; open).

**5. The heroes, the pet, enemies and NPCs are not camera obstacles (corrects the earlier drafts; High).** The list that earlier drafts called the region characters is the world's near-tree list: up to 15 foliage patches of key 4 (SPEC-0060, the solid-trunk trees) that ran their logic this tick, each holding plants (0x70-byte records) that are tested as vertical cylinders. The list is emptied at the start of each world zone pass and refilled by the patches' own logic, which runs only while the camera eye is within 100 of the patch and the game state is below 9. It holds no character, so the camera collides with no hero, pet, enemy or NPC in either test. A character only meets the camera through the fade ramp (see-through below 5, invisible below 1.7 eye-to-focus distance). Tree trunk rules:
- Shape: a vertical cylinder from the plant's base, height 25, radius 5.5 times the plant's scale (0.85 to 1.0, drawn when the world is built). The eye pass pushes the eye out of it as a sphere of radius 1.25 (the same contact list, side-swing and failure rules as the other routines; no class or mask gate). The sight-line sweep treats each plant as a horizontal circle (height ignored; High) and skips a plant whose base is 50 or more horizontally from the eye position. The occlusion clip instead treats each plant as a vertical capped cylinder from its base up by the patch height (height matters: a line passing over the top is clear) with the same 50-unit skip. In the eye pass the plant is a cylinder-as-sphere contact: the contact shape is a kind 3 cylinder with axis (0, 1, 0), the plant's position with its w lane replaced by the patch's radius lane, and the patch's height stored as its length (Medium-High: the shape field meanings are read from the cylinder primitive layout).
- Radius quirk (Medium-High): all three camera tests (eye pass, clip, sweep) take the radius and the height from the patch's shared trunk fields (read once per patch, applied to every plant), not from the plant; that cylinder's radius was last written for the last plant the patch processed (that last part is Medium; why the patch holds the last plant's value is not established). In a patch whose plants have different scales every plant is tested with the last plant's radius.
- Test vector (Medium on the margin): plant at (0, 0), patch radius 5.5 x 0.9 = 4.95, base y 40. An eye at (3, 45, 0) is inside the trunk and its height and is pushed out horizontally to 4.95 + 1.25 = 6.2 from the axis. An eye at y 70 (above 40 + 25 plus the margin) is not touched. The segment from the eye (-20, 45, 0) to the focus (20, 45, 0) is blocked; with the eye at (-55, 45, 0) the plant is 55 from the eye, so it is skipped and the segment is clear.

**6. Gated walls (High for the gate rule and the sight-line behaviour).** A frame-record entry with a nonzero gate word (a single bit 1, 2, 4 or 8; 37 part-only entries in packs 696, 699, 702, 706, 714, 753, 880, 886 and 1066, SPEC-0018 and SPEC-0042) is switched on by the world's collision word, which the pack's module sets with SetWorldCollisMask (SPEC-0042, World mask call sites). The entry is used when its gate is 0 or the gate AND the word is nonzero, and the word is 0 at every world set-up, so a gated wall is off until a module sets a bit. The wall rings of packs 696 and 714 turn on (word 4) only while flag 220 is set and flag 284 is clear; the four-panel entries of packs 702, 753, 880 and 886 turn on (word 1) only when flag 252 is clear at Init; the others follow their flags in the SPEC-0042 table. The two camera tests differ:
- The eye pass (push-out) compares the entry's gate with the collision word of the camera's ground world (above). A gated-off wall does not push the eye.
- The sight-line test reads no gate. It tests the frame records of the end-point cells (and of the camera-position cell in the sweep) against the segment whatever the entry's gate says, so a gated-off wall still blocks the line of sight, feeds the 60-tick timer and ends in the recentre pass on the 61st blocked tick. Placed-object entries never have a gate, so this concerns only the 37 level entries. Test vector: a wall ring entry with gate 4, world word 0, the hero inside the ring and the eye outside it: the ring does not push the eye, but the line through it is blocked, and after 61 ticks the recentre pass runs. With word 4 the ring pushes the eye 1.25 out and blocks the line as well. The gated volumes (44 on disc, in list A) use the primitive mask gate of the routines above, and the sight line reads that gate against the current world's word.

**7. Arrival measurement for placed objects (Medium; static data, each object at its rest and end pose, the state at load not applied).** All 174 module arrival rows (86 packs) were tested against every placed-object wall record: the line from the hero's focus to the eye that the call places (distance and angle of SetToNormalCamera, eye at the focus height as the pose setter makes it) and the 1.25 margin around that eye. Placed-object walls start within 12 horizontal units of the hero in 8 rows only, and cross the line or the margin in one: pack 1411 value 10 (lift object 0, 12.2 away; placed 19.88 up by InitObjOn(0, true) when the module selects the end pose; at that pose its 1.2 by 13.6 column crosses the 10.0 line and holds the eye within the margin; it is the same obstacle as the box wall of the earlier census, group COLL). The other seven rows: pack 880 value 80 (objects 0 and 1, 6.4 and 6.5 behind; sliding doors), pack 909 value 10 (object 8, 7.4 behind), pack 1023 value 106 (object 5, 11.7), pack 1174 start (objects 1, 73 and 74 at 9 to 11; angle pi), pack 1300 value 20 (objects 0 and 1 at 11.5) and value 60 (objects 0, 1 and 2 at 8.5, 4.4 and 8.5; the arrival makes no placement call), pack 1335 value 20 (objects 0 and 1, 4.1 and 6.1 behind). None is in the line or the margin, so a scripted arrival never starts with the eye in a placed wall. Engine placements at entry 0 (eye 5 or 10 behind) in the nine Act 2 packs: no placed wall in the line or the margin (placed objects within 12 only in pack 1174, no hit). Door-rule landings that put the eye on the door plane (landing minus 5 along the plane normal, hero facing the normal): pack 880 door 0 has the eye in the sliding leaves' walls at their start pose, and pack 1300 door 0 has object 0's wall in the margin and across the line; the other four placed doors have no placed wall in the way. With the facing reversed (the hero arrives facing the door) all six are clear.

**8. Act 2 packs and others: door objects near the arrival ("walls" are placed-object frame records).**
| Pack | Placed objects with walls | Near the arrivals | Can they block or push the eye? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1300 | 0 and 1 (50 records each, 133 deep, x -214 to -46, z -143 to 13; object 1 is used while game state 0x252 is clear, objects 0 and 2 when it is set), 2 (27 records, x -222 to -184, z -23 to 12); 4 and 5 have no walls | value 20 locator 7: 0 and 1 at 11.5; value 60 locator 6: 1 at 4.4; none at entry 0 | not at any scripted placement; a door-plane landing at door 0 is pushed and blocked by object 0 (Medium) |
| 1327 | 15 objects, some indices computed | none within 12 of locators 27 and 28 or entry 0 | no |
| 1335 | 0 (15 records) and 1 (4 records, at door 0), chosen by game state 0xF1 | value 20 locator 1: 4.1 and 6.1 behind | no (eye in front); a door-plane landing at door 0 is clear |
| 1344 | 0 to 5, all moving (platforms and doors) | none near entry 0 (plain load) | no |
| 1324 | 16, 18 and 20 move | none within 12 of entry 0 | no |
| 1299 | none | none | no |
| 1098 | 2, 3, 8 and 10 to 13 move (platforms); object 6 owns a ladder group | none within 12 of the nine arrival locators or entry 0 | no; its doors are level mesh plus door rectangles |
| 1174 | 0, 1, 22 and 24 move; 73 and 74 static | start: 1, 73, 74 at 9 to 11 | no hit |
| 886 | none | none | no |
| 880 | 0 to 3, sliding double doors (0 and 1 shown) | value 80 locator 2: 6.4 to 6.5 behind | arrival eye in front: no; a door-plane landing: pushed |
| 909 | 8 static (and others) | value 10 locator 2: 7.4 behind | no |
| 1023 | 5 lift | value 106 locator 16: 11.7 | no |
| 1139 | 0 sliding | none near locators 0 and 1 | no |
| 1411 | 0 lift | value 10 locator 1: 12.2 | yes, at its end pose (group COLL) |
| 722, 991, 1056, 1014 | none near any locator | none | no |

**Test vectors for objects.**
- Door hidden, then shown: a panel with run length 3.0, normal (1, 0, 0), plane x = 10, the eye moving from 11.5 to 10.4 inside the panel's windows. Hidden (run length -200): no hit, the eye stays at 10.4. ActivateObj called before the next camera pass: hit, depth 0.85, the eye ends at 11.25.
- Moving wall: the same panel moving +0.28 per tick, the eye stationary at x = 11.0: after tick 1 the plane is at 10.28, dP = 0.72, depth 0.53, the eye is at 11.53; after tick 2 the plane is at 10.56 and the eye at 11.81 (the eye rides 1.25 in front of the plane).
- Wall moving across an eye that is behind it: the plane moves -0.4 per tick from x = 10 toward an eye stationary at x = 9.2: tick 1 plane 9.6 (dP -0.4, no hit); tick 2 plane 9.2 (dP 0, no hit); tick 3 plane 8.8 (dP 0.4, hit, depth 0.85, the eye ends at 10.05). A plane that stops at 9.6 leaves the eye behind it.

**Rendering (see SPEC-0046).** The near plane is at 1.0 and the focal length 512 (horizontal field of view 64.0 degrees, vertical 50.2 at 640 x 480). A box wholly nearer than the near plane is culled; a box that crosses it is drawn with per-triangle clipping, so geometry cut by the near plane is clipped, not dropped. There is no back-face removal, so an eye inside a wall shows the wall's back side rather than seeing through it. Nothing but the hero is faded or hidden for being between the eye and the hero (the fade ramp above); scenery is not alpha-faded and there is no hide-by-distance rule for the near side. Which parts of the level draw is decided by the zone row, not by the eye position: in mode 0 with a follow target the row is the zone of the target's ground entry, and in every other mode (and during a scripted play) it is the zone of the camera's own ground entry, found by the downward probe under the eye. Test vector (visibility matrix rows 2 = [1, 1] and 3 = [1, 0], hero in zone 2, eye over ground of zone 3): mode 0 reads row 2 and draws column 1; a scripted play or mode 8 reads row 3 and hides column 1. When the current world is the interior buffer, the other world is not drawn at all, so from inside an interior the outside world cannot show through the door, whatever the eye does.

### Eye pass per-primitive routines (Confidence: High that they belong to the camera; High for the frame-record routine, the sphere routine, the box push and the common pieces; Medium for the cylinder, zero-radius and tree-trunk steps)

The eye pass has five per-primitive routines that share one entry pattern (SPEC-0018 points here for them; they serve the camera, not the characters), plus the box push. The moving body is the camera eye (0xE0). Which routine handles what (High):
| Routine | Handles | Class bits it reads (+0x12) | Posts the side-swing event |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Frame-record routine | ground frame records of the eye's cell (type 6 skipped) | none | yes |
| Sphere routine | cell list A primitives of kind 1 (spheres: centre +0x30, radius +0x3C, grown by 1.25) | 0, 1 and 2 | yes |
| Cylinder routine | cell list A primitives of kind 3 | 0, 4 and 5 | yes |
| Zero-radius routine | kinds 4 and 7 with the radius value +0x2C at or below 0 | 0 and 5 | yes |
| Tree-trunk routine | each plant of each patch in the near-tree list | none | yes |
Kind 2 (cone) and every other kind run no routine. Every routed primitive routine first applies the primitive mask gate in step 1. Its class gate is then: kind 1 requires class 0 or 1 and rejects class 2; kind 3 requires class 0 or 4 and rejects class 5; zero-radius kinds 4 and 7 require class 0 or 5; positive-radius kinds 4 and 7 require class 0 or 3. The tree-trunk routine has no class-bit gate. The moving-object push uses the same geometric helper as positive-radius boxes but enters through a separate call path that does not apply the primitive mask or class gate. These are the complete static class predicates for the live eye-pass dispatch (High).
2. Class gate. The applicable predicate is the one for the dispatched primitive route listed above. A failed predicate returns with no eye change.
3. Contact test. The routine tests the eye as a sphere of radius 1.25 (0x2DB400) against the primitive: for kind 1 the eye point against the sphere grown by 1.25 (distance below r + 1.25), a cylinder test for kind 3, and for the zero-radius kinds a sweep from the eye along its per-tick velocity (the spring displacement at +0x110 divided by the physics-block float at +4; confirmed 2026-11-09, High). With no contact it returns: nothing changes and the failure flag is not touched. The zero-radius routine hands primitives whose own axis at +0x44 has |y| strictly above 0.85 (decided from the primitive before any contact test; corrected 2026-11-09: it is not the hit normal) to a separate floor and ceiling push that moves the eye out along the hit normal; that path posts no side-swing event and sets no failure flag (Medium).
4. Contact. The hit normal N is added to the contact list (a normal within 0.999 of a stored one refreshes that entry; at most 19 entries) and the eye is moved to the eye plus N times the contact depth. When the previous failure, the failure flag or the restore latch is already set (see Eye pass failure flag), the routine stops here.
5. Settling (High for the frame-record routine, the sphere routine and the box push, read from the code 2026-11-08; High for the cylinder and zero-radius routines, read in full 2026-11-09; Medium for the tree-trunk routine). The routines do not share one settling rule. Common pieces:
- Nudge: the eye is moved along a direction by the contact depth only when the focus hero is moving or the hold word (0x26C) is set, and the stuck counter is 0, and the direction has a positive dot product (xyz, strictly above 0) with every normal already in the contact list (an empty list passes).
- Side probe in a direction V: the segment of length 10.0 (0x2DB3FC) from the eye along V must be clear of the sweep test, and then the sight line from the far end of it to the focus (the line-of-sight sweep) is consulted. The probe along D accepts when the segment is clear and that sight line is NOT blocked. The probe along minus D is not its mirror: it accepts when the segment is clear and that sight line IS blocked (the test is inverted; probably an original slip, but it is what the game does; High for the code, Medium for the intent).
- Resolution event: when one probe accepts and the hold word is zero, the side-swing event is posted to the camera with the rate 0.01 (0x2DB404), the accepted direction, the duration 10 ticks (0x2DB408) and the direction flag 1; with the hold word nonzero nothing is posted (the failure flag is not affected by the hold word). When neither probe accepts, the failure flag (0x508) is set to 1 and nothing else changes.
Per routine:
- Frame-record routine: the camera plane test (below) runs with radius 1.25 on the spring displacement as the start point; on a hit the normal is added to the contact list and the eye moved out by depth along it (w = 1); if the previous failure, the failure flag or the latch is set the routine stops (returns 1). The clear test is a segment from a start point (the focus lowered by the absolute value of the vertical component of the record's top-plane normal; only the first two lanes of the focus come from the getter, so the start point's other two lanes are whatever the caller left, see Open questions) to the eye against the record's rectangle, a two-sided rectangle crossing. D is the record's own run axis (not its face normal). If the segment does NOT cross (clear): D is reversed when D . heading (0x100) is not above 0, and the nudge runs along the result. If it crosses: the probe along D, then along minus D, as above, with the event or the failure. At the end, when the focus is moving or the hold word is set and no failure or latch flag is set, the hit normal's component is removed from the spring velocity (v = v - n (v . n)), so the eye slides along the wall (High for this routine). Return value 1 when a flag was already set after the first push, else 0.
- Sphere routine (kind 1): gates as in the table; the contact is the 3D distance from the eye to the sphere's centre below 1.25 + radius, normal unit(eye - centre), depth 1.25 + radius - distance, eye moved by normal x depth; stops if a flag is already set. The clear test is the eye-to-focus segment against that sphere taken as a horizontal circle of the sphere's radius (the sight-line sphere test, with the current world's mask and the same class gates), not the camera's own shape sweep. D = the horizontal unit perpendicular to the line from the eye to the centre: normalise(unit(eye -> centre) x up) = (-uz, 0, ux) for up = (0, 1, 0). When the segment is blocked: the probe along D then along minus D (D not reversed), event or failure. When it is clear: D is reversed by the heading (D . heading not above 0 gives minus D) and the nudge runs. The reversal exists only on this clear branch.
- Box push (kinds 4 and 7 with a positive third extent, and the movable blocks): not one of the five routines; it never fails and never posts the event. Gates (skipped when the caller says so, as the movable blocks do): the mask word, then class bit 3 or class bit 0. The eye's w is set to 1, then the face choice of SPEC-0018 (Point versus box with radius r, Six faces and Face rules) runs with eye radius 1.25 and the top-growth flag set (the camera passes it; the characters do not): the inside test is -r < a < eA + r, -r < b < eB + r, -r < v < eV - 0.1 + r; the face depths, bias, top gate, corner rule and the 0.8 r / 0.7 r / 2 r reconsideration are those of SPEC-0018; faces 1 and 2 are never reconsidered; no preferred face is used. Pushed distances: top face depth + r (the depth carries the -0.1, so a top push leaves the eye 0.1 inside r above the top), bottom face |depth + 0.5 r| (the eye ends r + 0.125 below the bottom plane), other faces depth + r; normals as in SPEC-0018. On a hit the normal is added to the contact list, the eye moved out and w = 1; if a flag is already set the routine stops. Slide direction S from the face: faces 1 and 4 +axis A, faces 2 and 3 -axis A, face 5 -axis B, face 6 +axis B (A the axis with extent at +0x28, B the one with extent +0x2C of the primitive; the horizontal edge direction, not the push normal). The clear test is the eye-to-focus segment swept against this one box only (current world's mask, class bits 0 or 3): when it is blocked the routine ends with no further action (no probes, no failure flag, no event); when it is clear the nudge runs along S, reversed to minus S when S . heading is not above 0. So the box route only moves the eye out and, if the line to the focus is clear, nudges it sideways.
- Cylinder and zero-radius routines, read in full 2026-11-09 (High). The mask gate is the primitive's mask (u16 +0x1A) against the collision word (+0x56B4) of the camera's ground world; the classes are as in the table (cylinder: class 0 or 4, class 5 rejects; zero-radius: class 0 or 5). The wall clear test of the cylinder routine and the box routine's sweep gate use the game's current world mask instead (the same word in practice, a different path).
  - Zero-radius routine (rectangles of kind 4 or 7 with +0x2C at or below 0, door panels): if |axisB.y| of the primitive (its axis at +0x44) is strictly above 0.85, the primitive goes to the floor and ceiling push and nothing else runs; this is decided from the primitive's own axis before any contact test (corrected: it is not the hit normal's y). Otherwise the contact test is the slab contact depth (radius 1.25, the eye, the step = the spring displacement at +0x110 divided by the physics-block float at +4, the primitive, one-sided flag 1); no contact: done. Contact: the normal is added to the contact list, the eye moves by normal × depth (all four lanes), w = 1; stop if a flag is already set. Then the clear test: "eye-clear-of-primitive" returns nonzero when the eye-to-focus segment crosses the primitive (BLOCKED; its name is inverted). Blocked: try the side probe along +axisA (the axis at +0x50) with the forward move step, else along −axisA with the alternate move step; neither accepted: the failure flag (+0x508) = 1; accepted and the hold word is 0: the side-swing event is posted (rate 0.01, direction axis, 10 ticks, flag 1). Clear: the nudge along axisA when dot(heading +0x100, axisA) > 0.0, else along −axisA (a dot of exactly 0 takes the negative axis). Finally (after w = 1 and a second flag check that stops the routine) the spring displacement loses its component along the contact normal and is rescaled to its original length (unit(step − (step · n) n) × |step|; the fourth lane becomes the projected length × |step|); this step has no focus-moving or hold-word gate.
  - Cylinder routine (kind 3): eye w = 1; contact test = the eye as a sphere of radius 1.25 against the flat-ended cylinder (the sphere-against-flat-ended-cylinder routine of SPEC-0018); no contact: done. Contact: the normal is added, the eye moves by normal × depth, w = 1, stop if a flag is already set. The slide axis is D = unit(cross(unit(base − eye), up)) with base the vector at +0x30 of the primitive. The clear test is the eye-to-focus segment against the cylinder (below). Blocked: probe +D, then −D as above, with the event or the failure. Clear: the nudge along D when dot(heading, D) > 0.0, else along −D. There is no spring-displacement projection in this routine. Clear test of the cylinder routine: the segment from the eye to the focus (+0x120) against the cylinder primitive. The class gate uses the game's current world mask. The camera position (+0x30) and the cylinder's base (+0x30) must have a squared horizontal distance strictly below 2500.0 (50 units); then the line against the finite flat-ended cylinder (length at +0x24, radius = base's fourth lane, axis +0x40, centre = base + axis × length / 2) gives 0, 1 or 2 parameters: one root counts when 0 < t < the segment length (both strict); two roots count when the first or then the second lies strictly inside (0, length); no root: clear.
  - The nudge helper (contact normal nudge): does nothing when (the focus object's class query is 0 and the hold word at +0x26C is 0) or the stuck counter at +0x510 is nonzero; otherwise every recorded contact plane (count at +0x500, 0x20 bytes apart from +0x280) must have a strictly positive dot (3 lanes) with the new direction; the first plane whose dot is at most 0.0 (zero included) cancels the whole nudge; when all pass the eye (+0xE0) moves by direction × amount on all four lanes and w = 1.
  The tree-trunk routine tests the plant as a cylinder with the patch's shared length and radius, treated as a sphere contact of the eye.
Test vectors (synthetic): a sphere contact of depth 0.2 with the hero standing and the hold word 0: the eye moves out by 0.2 along N, and when the clear test passes nothing else happens; the same contact with the hero walking, a failing clear test, D clear and the hold word 0: the event is posted with rate 0.01, 10 ticks and flag 1; with the hold word 1 it is not posted; with D and minus D both blocked the failure flag is 1 and the eye has only been moved out along N.

### Side-swing event (Confidence: High)

The event takes a rate k, a vector N, a duration n (ticks) and a direction flag.
- Flag 0: the side-swing increment (0x1C8) = k and the counter (0x1CC) = n.
- Flag 1: only when N . F > 0, with F the camera's forward row (0x20): the increment = k, negated when N . R < 0 (R the camera's first row, 0x00); the counter = n. When N . F is at or below 0 nothing is written.
Each following follow tick adds the increment to the spin value I while the counter runs down (Spin), so the eye swings sideways about the focus for n ticks and then the extra rate decays with the spin decay. Every poster uses the flag 1 and the rate 0.01.
Posters: the five eye pass routines above (10 ticks); two character contact routines and one hero routine of the motion code (30 ticks each), among them the general case of the hero's ledge hand-off (SPEC-0018), which posts only when the hero's word +0x148 is set.
Test vector (exact arithmetic; spin input D = 0, I = 0 at the start, flag 1 event with N . F > 0 and N . R >= 0, k = 0.01, n = 10): I after ticks 1, 2 and 3 is 0.01, 0.01875 and 0.0264063 (I <- 0.875 I + 0.01); after tick 10 it is 0.0589539 (0.08 x (1 - 0.875^10)); from tick 11 on it decays by 0.875 per tick. With N . R < 0 the same values are negative. With N . F <= 0 nothing changes.
Failure is the last case of step 5: neither probe direction is clear, so the failure flag (0x508) is set to 1 and nothing else is written. The flag's effect is below.

### Eye pass failure flag (Confidence: High for the flag, the counter, the limit and which primitive kinds each routine handles)

The per-primitive routines of the follow camera's eye pass (above) share five words of the camera record: the contact-normal count (0x500), the previous failure (0x504), the failure flag (0x508), the restore latch (0x50C) and the stuck counter (0x510). The camera's contact-block reset clears the previous failure, the failure flag, the restore latch and the stuck counter (and the line-of-sight words 0x514 to 0x51C, the side offset and target, the side lean, the hold counter, both biases, both turn leans, the ground world and the ground height, and sets the focal-length word at 0xC0 to 512), but it does not write the contact-normal count (0x500); entries leave that list only by ageing out.
1. At the start of each eye pass: nothing at all happens when the camera's ground-world word (0x1D8) is null (the flags and counters are then untouched); otherwise the contact list ages and failure = 0. The four sources then run in order (movable blocks, the cell's frame records, list A, trunks; frame records first, then list A) and the timers run after them.
2. A per-primitive routine that cannot resolve its contact sets failure = 1. While any of the previous failure, the failure or the latch is set, the later routines of the same pass skip their resolution step.
3. At the end of the pass:
- if failure = 0 and latch = 0: counter = 0;   - otherwise: if the contact count is at least 2 or the latch is set, the eye is put back at the previous eye (0xF0) and latch = 1; then counter = counter + 1;   - if the counter is now greater than (int)(0.05 x tick rate) = 3 (0.05 from 0x2DB3E8, times the run-time tick-rate variable, 60; the game has no fixed 60), the camera recovery routine (the recentre pass) runs and the counter, the latch, the failure and the previous failure are all cleared.
4. Previous failure = failure, as it stands after step 3 (so it is 0 after a recovery).
After step 4 the line-of-sight timer runs (Walls and the stuck timers); the stuck timer is first, and the final eye is stored to 0xF0 after both.
So recovery runs only after more than 3 consecutive failing ticks.

### Recentre pass and R3 (Confidence: High for the trigger, the candidate order, the shrink, the commit and the constants; confirmed from the code 2026-11-08)

**Trigger.
** The pass runs on the R3 press edge (bit 0x400 of the player's press-edge word 0x154) when the latch (0x1D0) is 0, and from the two timers of Walls and the stuck timers. The latch is released (set to 0) on a tick when it is 1 and bit 0x400 of the held word (0x150) is clear, that is once R3 is released.
**Start.
** First, if the selected hero exists and its target-override flag (hero record +0x15CC, read as 0 while the boot word of Hold and lean terms is nonzero) is nonzero, one hero word (+0xDC0) is copied to another (+0xF68). Then latch = 1, manual flag (0xC8) = 0; the eye (0xE0, not the position word 0x30) is remembered; the focus is recomputed from the focus object.
1. **Behind.
** d = the target distance (profile distance x (1 - 0.65 sin^2 e) plus the side offset 0x1E0, with e taken from the current polar 0x140, not from the candidate). P = polar(-d x facing), with facing the focus character's facing vector; then P.e = the profile default elevation (profile +8) and t_e = that elevation. Candidate eye = F + sphere(P). The spring velocity (0x110) is set to (0, 0, 0, 1) here, unconditionally, on every pass, before any test. The candidate is blocked when the world segment query (restriction 1) from it to F returns a hit OR the buried test (below) fires. If it is clear it is kept and the front step is skipped.
2. **In front.
** Only when the behind candidate is blocked: the raw test point is F + d x facing (same d, at focus height). If it is clear (same two tests), P = the polar of (point - F) with the elevation replaced by the profile default, and the candidate is F + sphere(P): this elevated point is the used eye and is not tested again; the shrink is skipped. If the front point is blocked too, the BEHIND candidate and its polar are restored and the shrink starts from the behind candidate.
3. **Shrink.
** Entered only when the candidate distance r (the measured distance of the candidate from F) is above 1.0 and the front candidate was not taken. Each pass: test the current candidate (the two tests below); if clear, stop; if blocked, r = r - 0.25, the polar radius = r, the candidate is rebuilt on the same yaw and elevation and the reseat flag (0x198) is set to 1. The loop continues while r > 1.0. The 1.0 is a loop guard, not a floor: a pass runs only while r is above 1.0 and a blocked pass then lowers r by a full 0.25, so the last radius can be anywhere in (0.75, 1.0] (a start of 1.1 or 4.1 ends at 0.85) and the last shrunk candidate is not tested. When the behind candidate was clear and its distance is above 1.0 the loop retests it once (redundant) and stops.
4. **Commit.
** Position (0x30), eye (0xE0) and previous eye (0xF0) = the candidate (w = 1); the eye motion direction (0x100) = (0, 0, 0, 1); then the OCCLUSION CLIP (Camera on world entry, Occlusion clip) runs on the position word. It is not the per-tick wall push; it reads the geometry of the world in the camera's ground-world word (0x1D8) and does nothing when that word is 0. The eye = the clipped position. If the 3D eye-to-focus distance is at least 0.1, camera reset (see Camera reset) runs with the clipped eye and the focus and the pass ends.
5. **Second pass** (the clipped eye is closer than 0.1 to F). The eye, previous eye and position word = the remembered eye; 0x100 and 0x110 = (0, 0, 0, 1) again; the front candidate ONLY (the elevated point from the polar with the default elevation, no preliminary line test), then the shrink loop as in step 3 (its first test is on that candidate), then the commit and clip as in step 4. If the clipped eye is still closer than 0.1 to F, the eye, previous eye and position word are put back to the remembered eye and 0x100 = (0, 0, 0, 1). Camera reset then always runs (with that eye and the focus), so a reset happens at the end of every recentre pass. The spring velocity is zero after every recentre pass (High; this is the only path that zeroes it besides the record's first build).
The two blocked tests (High for both descriptions; which world the buried probe reads is not established) are different tests on the same candidate, and either one blocks:- Line test: the segment query of SPEC-0018 between the focus and the candidate eye, with the restriction flag set, so two-sided walls (frame type 6) are ignored. Any hit blocks. It reads the current world, never the camera's ground world.
- Buried test: a ground probe (SPEC-0018) at the candidate eye's x and z with a ceiling 5.0 above the focus height. It blocks when the height returned is more than 5.0 above the focus height. That happens only through the probe's fallback, when every ground surface there lies above the ceiling, so the candidate is under a hill or ledge that rises more than 5 units above the focus.
Test vectors (buried test): focus (0, 2, 0), candidate eye at x 0, z -9.55 (ceiling 7.0). Only ground at height 8.0 there: the probe returns 8.0, which is above 7.0, so the candidate is blocked and shrinks. Ground at 6.5 (and 8.0): 6.5 is accepted, so the candidate is not blocked by this test.
Test vectors (shrink):- Distance 6.0, segment blocked for r > 3.0 (clear at 3.0). Tested distances: 6.0, 5.75, 5.5, ..., 3.0 (13 tests, 12 shrinks). Final distance 3.0, length at least 0.1, so the pass commits and resets.
- Distance 4.0, always blocked: tests at 4.0, 3.75, ..., 1.25 (12 tests, 12 shrinks). The loop stops at 1.0 without another test. Final distance 1.0.
- Distance 1.25, always blocked: one test at 1.25, shrink to 1.0, loop ends. Final 1.0, no test at 1.0.
- Distance 1.0 or less: no test and no shrink.
- Distance 1.1, always blocked: one test at 1.1, shrink to 0.85, loop ends (0.85 is not above 1.0). Final 0.85, no test at 0.85. Distance 4.1: tests at 4.1, 3.85, ..., 1.1 (13 tests, 13 shrinks), final 0.85.
- Distance 0.05 (below the 0.1 commit length): no shrink; the clipped length 0.05 is below 0.1, so the second pass runs (front candidate only, then its shrink, commit and clip); if that is still below 0.1 the remembered eye is restored, the velocity and eye motion direction are zeroed and camera reset still runs.

### Pose setter with pull-in (set-pose rule, not per tick) (Confidence: High for the rule)

The pull-in setter is the camera object's pose hook. It is used by the boot and world-load attaches, the restart placement and the three return-to-normal script calls (see Placement after a load or restart and Scripted cameras), not per tick. Given a wanted eye E:1. The focus point F is recomputed (the follow focus).
2. The eye height is replaced by the focus height: E' = (E.x, F.y, E.z). This happens in every case, so the wanted eye's own height is never kept.
3. The ground is probed at (E.x, E.z) from F.y + 5.
0. If the ground there is higher than F.y + 5.0, the horizontal offset is halved ONCE (a single halving, not a loop): E' = (F.x + 0.5 (E.x - F.x), F.y, F.z + 0.5 (E.z - F.z)), computed as the unit horizontal direction times the length times 0.5. The factor 0.5 and the probe height 5.0 are immediates of the code, not boot data words (no data word is read; the earlier mention of a data word for the 0.5 is withdrawn).
4. E' (with w = 1) is written to the camera position (0x30), the eye (0xE0) and the previous eye (0xF0); the eye motion direction (0x100) is cleared to (0, 0, 0, 1).
5. The occlusion clip runs on the camera position (see Camera on world entry, Occlusion clip). It is not the per-tick eye pass: it pulls the eye back along the line from the focus, and it reads the geometry of the world stored in the camera's ground-world word (0x1D8), which is the world the camera last probed, not necessarily the world that has just become current. The eye (0xE0) is then set to the clipped position; the previous eye (0xF0) keeps the unclipped E' until the reset below overwrites it with the clipped eye. The focus is recomputed and camera reset runs with the eye and the focus. The setter does not write the spring velocity (0x110), the contact list (0x280 and the count 0x500) or the stuck and line-of-sight counters (High; confirmed from the code 2026-11-08).
Because of step 2 the eye starts level with the focus (elevation 0); the follow chase then raises it toward the profile elevation over the next ticks.
Test vectors: focus (0, 2, 0), wanted eye (0, 4.5, -10). Ground at (0, -10) at 6.0: eye (0, 2, -10). Ground there at 9.0: eye (0, 2, -5).

### Camera reset (Confidence: High for the writes; confirmed from the code 2026-11-08)

Camera reset takes the eye and the focus it is given (not read from the record) and writes, in this order: the eye motion direction (0x100) = (0, 0, 0, 1); the eye (0xE0) and the previous eye (0xF0) = the eye; the reseat flag (0x198) = 1; the polar P of (eye - focus); the side-swing counter (0x1CC) = 0; the current polar (0x140) = P; the manual elevation (0xC4) = P's elevation; the target polar (0x130) = P; the elevation target (0x154) = P's elevation; the spin value I (0x150) = 0. Callers: the scripted camera, the pose setter, the recentre pass, the eye snap and the mode reset.
It does NOT write the spring velocity (0x110; it lives there, not in 0x100), the focus point (0x120), the position word (0x30), the ground entry, world and height (0x1D4, 0x1D8, 0x1DC), the side-swing increment (0x1C8), the manual flag (0xC8), the R3 latch (0x1D0), or the contact list and counters (0x500 to 0x510).
It does not write the spin input D (0x264), the stick value (0x268) or the increment (0x1C8). D is recomputed every tick, so no stale value persists beyond the next tick. Reset runs after a level reset, after a return-to-normal, after a recentre commit and after each placement. The level reset (new game, quit, game over; SPEC-0026) itself first stops a running scripted camera play when one is active, then sets the camera's draw distance to 512.0 and rebuilds it (High, game code read 2026-11-08). Two readers of the camera record that touch no camera state: making a world current copies the camera's four pose quads and the point ahead of the camera to the music listener (SPEC-0006, Make current), and the world's own character block tick (every tick) copies the camera's first four quads (pose rows and position) to move the save-point glow emitters, reading the record after that pass's camera update. The world-load routines (make current, the post-load rule, the start-point load) never move the camera themselves; only the pet placement at the start pose, the start-point default camera and the interior jobs do, and the camera volumes are blended once at the end of the post-load rule, after make-current (SPEC-0006, Hero placement).

### Camera initialisation (Confidence: High)

When the camera is initialised, its offset is set to (0, 0, 50) and its forward direction to (0, 0, -1); both are fixed constants (50.0 and -1.0), not boot data words. Read together, the offset puts the eye 50 units along +Z from the focus and the forward vector points back at the focus along -Z (the frame convention is in Polar convention). The initialisation then attaches the camera to the player (see Placement after a load or restart). The camera's field reset at construction also sets the focal length to 512.

### Placement after a load or restart (Confidence: High)

On a restart the hero is put at the restart point P with facing f. The camera is posed at eye = P - 10 f + 2.5 (0, 1, 0), looking along f (right = normalise(up x f)). The pull-in setter then runs (see Pose setter with pull-in), which sets the eye to the focus height (so the 2.5 lift does not survive), then the occlusion clip and a camera reset (see Camera on world entry for the order of these against the hero placement and the world becoming current). The follow spring settles the camera in about 45 to 60 ticks (first test vector of Follow camera). Attaching the camera to a character (boot, new game, start point, hero switch) instead puts the eye at position - d x facing (d = the target distance at the current elevation, no lift); the boot and world-load attaches then run the pull-in setter, the hero switch does not (see below). The up vector used here is the run-time block at 0x3932E0, (0, 1, 0, 0).
**The attach in detail.
** The attach sets the focus object (0x70) to the character, poses the camera from the character's position and its locomotion facing, and sets the manual flag (0xC8), the side-swing counter (0x1CC), the R3 recentre latch (0x1D0) and the camera's ground entry (0x1D4) to 0. It takes a hook flag. With the flag 1 (the boot and world-load attaches) the camera object's own hook runs as part of the pose step; with the flag 0 the hook is skipped. The hook is the pull-in setter (the camera class entry that the restart placement and the return-to-normal calls also use). The camera reset runs in both cases. The attach computes a wanted eye at position - d x facing and hands it to the hook; the last step of every attach (flag 0 or 1) then runs the occlusion clip on the camera position word (0x30), copies that word to the eye and the previous eye, and runs camera reset from it. With the flag 1 the hook has just written the lifted, clipped eye to that word, so the result is the pull-in eye. With the flag 0 the hook was skipped, the word still holds the previous tick's eye (the follow tick writes it every tick), and that old eye, clipped, becomes the eye: the wanted eye is computed and then not used. So after a hero switch the eye does not move and is not lowered or pulled in, and after a boot or world-load attach it is level with the focus (High for the flag 1 case; Medium-High for the flag 0 case, from the code).**Hero switch (camera hand-off).
** On a hero switch (SPEC-0053 section 3.6b) the only camera action is this attach, made to the incoming hero at the swap's select step with the hook flag 0, followed by the camera reset.
