Then the followed player record (0x60), the camera's ground reference, is set to the incoming hero's ground-reference record (hero record +0x9A0). The incoming hero has already taken the outgoing hero's position and facing, and the eye keeps the place it had, so the view is the same one with a new focus object. There is no camera turn and no blend. When the swap's second flag is set, the white flash (SPEC-0053 section 3.5) is opaque at that moment and hides the cut. (High for the calls; Medium for the visible result.)Test vector: P (10, 0, 5), f (0.6, 0, 0.8), hero height H: the posed eye is (4, 2.5, -3); after the pull-in setter it is (4, H, -3), or (7, H, 1) when the ground at (4, -3) is higher than H + 5 (focus (10, H, 5), no bias).
Test vector (hero switch): outgoing hero at (10, 0, 5) facing (0.6, 0, 0.8), previous tick's eye (4, 3.9, -3), switch accepted: on the selection tick the camera's focus object and followed player record are the incoming hero's, the eye after the attach is (4, 3.9, -3) (the previous eye, unless the occlusion clip pulls it in), the manual flag and the R3 latch are 0, and no turn or blend runs on later ticks.

**Arrival through a scripted door (from the code and disc data; not run in the original; High for the calls and the formula, Medium-High for the tick values).** The attach and restart camera above is the camera of a load only when no script follows it. A level script's activate-world callback is started after the load's arrival placement (SPEC-0030, row 252) and can replace the camera with its own call. Pack 1098 does this for every return value that moves the hero: 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 60, 70 and 90 (locators 6, 5, 9, 8, 7, 4, 3 and 1; value 90 is posted by pack 1152's teleport index 1 before the load). It calls MoveHero(locator, probe on, control 1, face on), resets the value to -1 and calls SetToNormalCamera(5.0, 0.0, true) (SPEC-0045); it is never ReturnToNormalCamera. Value 100 moves the hero to locator 10 and calls SetToNormalCamera(2.0, 0.0, true) only; value 140 does nothing. With the ground of the top-level height patches, the wanted eye point is on the same floor at each of these locators, so the pull-in does not halve the offset (Medium-High). The restart rule above would put the eye at P - d x facing, which here is on the +z side, outside the door wall; the original instead puts the eye 5 units in front of the hero along his facing (-z), inside the corridor.

Test vector (pack 1098, locator 1: facing (0, 0, -1), first row (-1, 0, 0); the probe puts the feet on the floor at -128.1094; hero idle, sticks centred, camera height bias 0): feet (78.3881, -128.1094, -56.1511); focus F = feet + 3.5 up = (78.3881, -124.6094, -56.1511). SetToNormalCamera(5.0, 0.0, true): wanted eye = feet + 5 x (0, 0, -1) = (78.3881, -128.1094, -61.1511). The pull-in setter lifts it to the focus height, (78.3881, -124.6094, -61.1511); the ground probed at that x and z from F.y + 5 = -119.6094 is -128.1094, which is not above that, so the offset is not halved; the occlusion clip finds no face on the way to the focus and the reset gives polar (5.0, -pi/2, 0) about the focus, spin 0. The follow step then pulls the eye back toward the profile distance (x stays 78.3881): eye z after ticks 1 to 6 = -61.576, -62.142, -62.718, -63.250, -63.717, -64.116; eye y = -124.601, -124.581, -124.550, -124.508, -124.456, -124.397; distance 5.425, 5.991, 6.567, 7.099, 7.567, 7.968; elevation 0.0016, 0.0047, 0.0091, 0.0143, 0.0202, 0.0267. Tick 12: z -65.446, y -123.933. It settles at about z -65.7, y -122.7 (distance 9.74, elevation 0.2). The line of sight stays clear and no stuck count or recentre occurs. The floor under the eye (-128.109) leaves the ground clearance unchanged. Medium for "no wall contact on the first ticks": the cell's primitives give a nearest wall face 3.2 away at tick 12, but frame records of moving objects and tree trunks are not included; the corridor is 17 wide.

### Camera on world entry (Confidence: High for the call order, the placement formulas and the fields that are kept; Medium-High for the first-tick values; High for the occlusion clip's face test, group prefilter, source order, per-kind shapes and box face set; Medium-Low where marked. From the game code; not run in the original)

**Summary.** No world entry blends, interpolates or carries the old view over. Every entry path that places the hero also places the camera in the same script step, once, with the pose setter: the eye is put at a wanted point near the hero, set to the focus height, pulled in toward the hero by an occlusion clip, and the follow state is rebuilt from it (camera reset). From the next tick the normal follow (mode 0) takes over: the spring pulls the eye from that point toward the profile distance, and the ground clearance, the per-tick wall pass and the two timers act on it as in play. The third argument of SetToNormalCamera is not a camera flag: it only puts the hero in state 1 (free), after the camera has been placed. Two things are not reset by any entry and matter on the first ticks: the spring velocity and the contact-normal list.

**Pieces, in the order they run in an entry.**
1. Return to normal: stops any running scripted play; in modes 8, 9 and 10 only it also sets mode 0 and runs camera reset from the base point and the pivot (Return to normal). In mode 0 it places nothing. With its release flag it puts the selected hero in state 1.
2. Pose setter (Pose setter with pull-in): focus recomputed from the hero's current position, eye set to the focus height, ground check with the halving rule, eye written to the camera position word (0x30), the eye (0xE0) and the previous eye (0xF0), eye motion direction (0x100) cleared, occlusion clip (below), focus recomputed, camera reset.
3. Camera reset: current and target polar = polar(eye - focus); manual elevation (0xC4) and elevation target (0x154) = its elevation; spin I (0x150) = 0; side-swing counter (0x1CC) = 0; reseat flag (0x198) = 1.
4. The next camera tick runs the normal follow (Follow camera) if the play-active flag is set and the camera has a focus object. The render view (the three rows and the position word 0x30) is rebuilt at the end of that tick by the look-at step, so the first view drawn from the new eye is the one built on that tick. An entry made by the hero's door contact or by a script thread runs after the camera update of its own tick; an entry made by the transition driver runs after that tick's draw. Either way the view that is drawn in the entry tick is the one built before the placement; the first view built from the placed eye is the next tick's, and it already contains one follow pass (see Tick table below). Whether the new world is drawn in the entry tick, and with which view, depends on the path; the table states it per path.

**What each entry path does to the camera, in order** (H = hero placement, W = the new world becomes current, D = first draw from the new eye, which is the draw of the first camera tick after the entry):
| Path | Order | Camera effects |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Door or script call into an interior (enter-interior job) | fade-out starts; the transition buffer is released; the interior loads; W; H (hero moved to the landing, ground attached, state 1, pet moved); camera volumes blended; return to normal (release); pose setter with the wanted eye L - 5 G (L the landing, G its facing); wait for 5 consecutive idle loader passes; fade-in of 18 frames | the setter runs after W and H, but its clip reads the world in the camera's ground-world word (0x1D8), which still holds the world the camera was last above (the exterior), not the interior. The job is stepped twice in the tick of the placement (the exterior becomes the second world and its manager is stepped again), so the fade-in starts 4 ticks after the placement tick: 3 whole follow ticks run under the opaque fade, a fourth under 94 per cent cover (more if the loader is busy). The interior's activate-world callback (module arrival call) runs on the next tick, after that tick's follow pass, so its clip reads the interior (Tick table) |
| Return to the exterior (leave-interior job) | hero state 1; instant fade-out and one yield; W (the exterior); H (hero moved, state 1, pet moved); camera volumes blended; pose setter with the wanted eye P - 5 B (P the hero position, B its body forward); the interior and transition buffers are released and the named transition is loaded; wait for 5 idle passes; fade-in; return to normal (release) | the clip reads the interior (still the camera's ground world; it is released only after the setter). The exterior's activate-world callback (module arrival call) runs in the SAME tick, right after the job's slice, so its placement also clips against the interior. The final return to normal, in mode 0, places nothing |
| Teleport onto a plane of a world (a door into a resident world; a driver load whose request carries a plane index of 0 or more) | W; with the fade flag a fade to full and a fade to clear of 10 frames are requested in the same call and cancel (Medium); hero action state reset; H (position from the plane, or entry record 0); pet moved; camera volumes blended; pose setter with the wanted eye P - 5 B | no return-to-normal call and no state-1 write here. The clip reads the old camera ground world. A door contact does this inside the hero's step, so the new world is drawn in the same tick with the old view (one stale frame, no covering fade), and D is the next tick's picture. A driver load does it after the tick's draw, so no stale frame exists |
| Driver load with plane -1, -2 or a refused plane; script exterior replacement load; the reload after movies | H (hero moved to the start pose, or kept for -2); respawn placement: camera posed at P - 10 f + 2.5 up looking along f, then the pose setter on that eye; pet; one vertical-blank flush; then W when the request has the make-current word; camera volumes blended | here the setter runs BEFORE the world becomes current; its clip reads the old camera ground world, and so does its own ground check for the halving rule (that probe reads the current world, which is still the old one); the lift of 2.5 is discarded by the setter. In the other rows the world is already current when the setter's ground check runs |
| Streamed crossing (walking into the next buffer; world swap) | W only, with the zone-blend flag clear | the camera is untouched: no reset, no placement. The ground-world word follows the world under the eye on the next ground clearance |
| Movie hand-off | return to normal (release) while the old buffer is released; movies; reload through the driver load above with plane -2 (hero keeps his place) or -1 | the eye is rebuilt behind the hero at the end, as in the respawn placement |
| Load game | the default camera setup (mode 0, pose offset (0, 0, 50) with forward (0, 0, -1), profile record, focus on the selected hero with the attach and its pose setter), then at the end of the pass the saved pose is applied to the hero and the respawn placement runs | the final eye is the respawn placement's |
| New game, start point | worlds made current; the default camera setup; then the respawn placement with heal | as above |
| Act start, hero switch | the hero swap attaches the camera to the incoming hero with the hook flag 0 (Placement after a load or restart); a plain act start places no camera | the eye does not move |
| Level-script arrival calls (SetToNormalCamera, ReturnToNormalCamera with a locator) | run in the activate-world or start callbacks, after the placement of the load | they re-place the camera as in the rows above, using the hero's position at the time of the call |

**Tick table: order of events from the door trigger to the first visible frame (Confidence: High for the order inside a tick, for which tick writes the camera ground world and for the kept state; Medium-High for the double step of the enter-interior job and for the same-tick callbacks; Medium for the fade-in tick of the driver path. From the game code; not run in the original).**
Rules that the tables apply (one tick is one pass of the main loop, SPEC-0026):
1. Order inside a tick: (a) camera update: the follow pass runs the profile pick, stick, target, eye on the sphere, spring, ground clearance (writes the camera ground world), wall pass and the two timers, then the look-at step that rebuilds the camera rows and the position word from the eye, then the hero fade ramp from that eye; (b) the camera rows are copied to the renderer as this tick's view; (c) world update, including the hero's step (door contact); (d) script step (special threads in order: enter-interior job, leave-interior job, the activate-world callback; then the callbacks and control threads); (e) draw: the world that is current at this moment, with the view of step (b); (f) fade overlay step and draw (the level after this tick's update); (g) transition driver (driver loads, which run synchronously and draw nothing).
2. The picture of tick t is built from the eye that tick t's own follow pass produced. A placement made in (c), (d) or (g) of tick t is first drawn in tick t+1, after tick t+1's follow pass has already moved it once. "Follow tick 1" below is that first pass after a placement.
3. The camera ground world is written by the ground clearance of a follow pass (before that pass's wall pass; it probes the current world first, then the other resident world, and stores the supplier, else the current world) and, in modes other than 0 and during scripted plays, by the per-tick eye probe. Nothing else in the camera writes it. So a placement that runs before the first follow pass after a switch (every engine placement, and a same-tick callback) clips against the world that was current during the last follow pass. The wall pass of follow tick 1 already reads the new world. The sight-line test reads the current world only.
4. The pose setter's own ground check (the halving rule) reads the current world at the moment it runs: the new world in the jobs and in the plane landing, the old world in the respawn placement of a driver load.
5. The new world's activate-world callback is started by the switch itself, in the new world's script manager. It runs in the same tick when the switch is made inside the hero step (door contact: the script step then steps the new current world first), and in the same tick for the leave-interior job (the exterior's manager is the one running the job, and its activate-world slot comes after the job's slot in the same sweep). It runs on the next tick, after that tick's follow pass, when the switch is made by the enter-interior job (the interior's manager is not stepped in the switching tick) or by the transition driver (which runs after the script step).
6. Kept by every row: spring velocity, contact normals and count, stuck and line-of-sight counters, hold counter (Fields table). The contact list does not age while the hero is idle (the countdown runs only while the hero is in a moving state or the hold counter is nonzero) and no entry clears it. A running hero (door contact teleport) brings the running spring velocity (about the hero's speed along his run) and the contact list of that run; a hero who waited out a load (both jobs, state 1) brings a spring velocity relaxed by 0.4169 per tick (to about zero) and exactly the contact list he stopped with.
7. The hero's ground record is cleared by the switch, and a follow pass picks the camera profile only when the hero has one, so follow tick 1 normally uses the old world's profile (Fields table; Medium).
Table C: door contact into a resident world (teleport landing, fade flag, no covering fade). T0 is the tick of the contact.
| Tick | Current world | Camera ground world (push-out / clip) | Follow pass | Spring velocity, contacts | Drawn |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T0 (a) | old | written now: old | normal follow, old hero position | live | (picture not yet drawn) |
| T0 (c) | new (switched inside the landing) | old | none (already ran) | kept | hero placed at the plane landing, pose setter eye P - 5 B at the focus height, clip against the old ground world |
| T0 (d) | new | old | none | kept | the new world's activate-world callback runs now (first manager stepped is the new current world): MoveHero and SetToNormalCamera re-place the eye, clip against the old ground world |
| T0 (e) | new | old | - | - | screen fade none (the full and clear fade pair cancel); geometry of the NEW world seen through the view built before the move: one stale frame; hero fade level is the old one |
| T1 | new | written now: new, before the wall pass; push-out reads the new world | follow tick 1 on the placed eye (hero idle: stuck counter 0) | old velocity and contacts act now (worked example below) | new world; eye after follow tick 1; hero fade from this eye. First fully visible frame |
Table A: driver load (exterior replacement or a plane landing) followed by the arrival call of the module. M is the tick whose transition driver runs the load; the load itself is synchronous and draws nothing.
| Tick | Current world | Camera ground world (push-out / clip) | Follow pass | Spring velocity, contacts | Drawn |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M (g) | new (made current inside the landing, or at the end for the respawn placement) | old | none | kept | nothing; hero placed; engine pose setter clips against the old ground world (respawn placement: the halving ground check also reads the old world) |
| M+1 (a) | new | written now: new | follow tick 1 on the engine eye | kept, act now | - |
| M+1 (d) | new | new | none | - | the activate-world callback runs: MoveHero, then SetToNormalCamera (eye = feet + d x unit(forward cos a + first-row sin a), set to the focus height, clipped against the NEW ground world, polar reset, hero state 1). The call reads the hero position at that moment, so call-before-move (pack 1056) uses the position before the move |
| M+1 (e) | new | new | - | - | new world with the view of the engine eye (follow tick 1); covered by the load fade (opaque) in the exterior replacement path; with no cover this is a visible frame of the wrong camera |
| M+2 | new | new | follow tick 1 on the scripted eye | kept, act now | new world, scripted eye plus one spring step; first view of the scripted eye. Covered until the engine fade-in |
| about M+6 | new | new | follow tick 5 | - | the engine's 5-frame fade-in begins about 6 passes after the load (SPEC-0026 six-pass rule; Medium); levels 102.4, 76.8, 51.2, 25.6, 0 on the five updates |
Table B1: enter-interior job (door or StartEnterInterior). S is the job's first tick, R the tick on which it finds the interior loaded.
| Tick | Current world | Camera ground world (push-out / clip) | Follow pass | Spring velocity, contacts | Drawn |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S | exterior | exterior | yes (old world) | hero idle: velocity relaxes, contacts frozen | exterior; fade-out starts (update 1 of 18: 7.1 of 128) |
| S+1 to R-1 | exterior | exterior | yes | idem | exterior; the cover is complete after 18 or 19 updates, a time not tied to the load; a load shorter than that would switch worlds under a partly clear screen |
| R (d), first manager step | interior (switch inside the job) | exterior (written by R's follow pass) | none (ran earlier in R) | kept | hero moved, state 1, return to normal (no camera change in mode 0), pose setter eye L - 5 G, clip against the exterior grid: with interior coordinates this normally finds no cell, so no clip |
| R (d), second manager step | interior | exterior | - | - | the exterior is the second world and its manager is stepped again: the job runs idle pass 2 (pass 1 ran in the first step) |
| R (e) | interior | exterior | - | - | interior geometry with the view built before the placement; screen fully covered (128) |
| R+1 | interior | written now: interior | follow tick 1 | kept, act now | first call: the interior's activate-world callback runs after the follow pass (MoveHero, SetToNormalCamera: clip now reads the interior); job pass 3 |
| R+2, R+3 | interior | interior | follow ticks 2, 3 (or 1, 2 after a scripted re-placement) | spring working | covered; job passes 4 and 5 |
| R+4 | interior | interior | follow tick 4 | - | job leaves the loop and starts the 18-frame fade-in: this tick's picture has level 120.9 of 128, then 113.8, ... clear after 18 or 19 updates; the first picture seen is the eye after follow tick 4 (3 after the module's call) |
A busy loader pass at any pass resets the count and delays the fade-in tick by the same number of ticks; no other camera event happens meanwhile.
Table B2: leave-interior job. L is the job's first tick.
| Tick | Current world | Camera ground world (push-out / clip) | Follow pass | Spring velocity, contacts | Drawn |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L | interior | interior | yes | idle hero: contacts frozen | fade-out of 1 frame: this tick's overlay is already opaque |
| L+1 | interior | interior | yes | - | opaque; the job yields once more |
| L+2 (d) | exterior (switch inside the job) | interior (written by L+2's follow pass) | none (ran earlier in L+2) | kept | hero and pet placed, state 1, pose setter eye P - 5 B clipped against the interior (still the ground world); then the interior and the transition are released and the transition load starts; the exterior's activate-world callback runs later in the same sweep (MoveHero, SetToNormalCamera clip against the interior too) |
| L+2 (e) | exterior | interior | - | - | exterior with the view built before the placement; opaque |
| L+3 | exterior | written now: exterior | follow tick 1 | kept, act now | opaque |
| L+3 to F-1 | exterior | exterior | follow ticks | spring working | opaque while the transition loads and 5 non-busy passes pass |
| F | exterior | exterior | follow tick F-L-2 | - | 18-frame fade-in starts (120.9 first); then the return-to-normal call, which changes nothing in mode 0 and sets hero state 1 |
So both jobs show their first picture after several follow ticks: the eye has moved from the placed point toward the profile distance by then (0.915, 0.80179, 0.68644 of the gap after ticks 1 to 3; 0.486 after 5; 0.193 after 10), and a stale contact normal may have frozen it (worked example). Only a door contact (Table C) and a callback without a covering fade of its own show follow tick 1 itself.
Worked example (idle hero, carried velocity, carried contact normal; profile 0, flat floor, no walls so that only the stored state acts; hero feet (0, 0, 0) facing +z, SetToNormalCamera(5, 0): eye (0, 3.5, 5), focus (0, 3.5, 0); eye elevation 0, target distance 10, elevation target +0.01 per idle tick):
- Clean (velocity 0, empty list): eye z after follow ticks 1 to 6 = 5.425, 5.9908, 6.5671, 7.0985, 7.5654, 7.9653; velocity z = 0.425, 0.5659, 0.5763, 0.5313, 0.4670, 0.3999; eye y = 3.5085, 3.5283, 3.5597, 3.6017, 3.6530, 3.7124 (same shape as the pack 1098 vector). At rest (5.4356, 9.5501).
- Carried spring velocity (0.17, 0, 0) (a run along +x in the old world), empty list: x after ticks 1 to 6 = 0.0709, 0.1055, 0.1259, 0.1400, 0.1507, 0.1593, z as in the clean row to 0.001. The carried velocity decays by 0.4169 per tick and moves the eye in total 0.715 times its size (0.12) sideways: harmless.
- Stored normal (0, 0, -1) (a wall behind the eye in the old world, pointing back at the hero), velocity 0: on tick 1 the velocity z becomes 0.425, its dot with the normal is -0.425, not above 0, the hero is idle and the stuck counter is 0, so the eye does not move. It stays at (0, 3.5, 5) while the hero stands still; the velocity keeps growing (z 0.425, 0.602, 0.6756, 0.706, 0.7183, 0.7229 on ticks 1 to 6, 0.6998 and y 0.2896 on tick 30), nothing ages, no timer runs. If the hero starts walking on tick 31 the stored velocity is applied at once: eye (y, z) = (3.7896, 5.6998) on tick 31, then (4.0543, 6.3387), (4.2856, 6.8953), (4.4827, 7.3673) and so on to rest at (5.4356, 9.5501) about 60 ticks later; the stored normal ages out over 40 moving ticks.
- Carried velocity (0.17, 0, 0) with stored normal (-1, 0, 0): the velocity dot the normal is -0.0709 on tick 1 and stays negative while the x velocity decays toward 0, so the eye is frozen for as long as the hero idles although z velocity is positive. With stored normal (1, 0, 0) the dot is positive and the eye moves as in the carried-velocity row. Rule: the idle eye moves only when the velocity has a positive dot product with every stored normal; a normal at right angles to the velocity (dot exactly 0) pins too.
Pack 1056 (call before move; Table A with the call at M+1): the call places the eye 10 in front of entry record 0 and sets the focus from the hero position at the time of the call; MoveHero then moves the hero 61.6, 132.6 or 207.5 away in the same slice. Follow tick 1 (M+2) recomputes the focus at the hero's new position and keeps the eye where it was (the eye is put back on the sphere about the new focus with the same yaw), and the target is 10 away from the hero along that same direction. The spring then pulls the eye straight toward the hero's side in free flight (no swept test: only the cell under the eye is tested each tick, so walls between are passed unless a tick ends within 1.25 in front of a panel in the eye's cell, which pushes it to exactly 1.25). Horizontal eye-to-hero distance after follow ticks t (free flight, idle hero, no contacts): loc 4 (value 30, start 61.6): t=1 57.2, 2 51.4, 3 45.4, 5 35.1, 6 30.9, 10 20.0, 15 13.9, 20 11.5, 30 10.0, 45 9.6. Loc 2 (value 40, start 132.6): 122.2, 108.3, 94.2, 69.6, 59.8, 33.7 (t=10), 19.3, 13.5, 10.3, 9.6. Loc 3 (value 50, start 207.5): 190.7, 168.4, 145.6, 106.1, 90.2, 48.2 (t=10), 24.9, 15.8, 10.6, 9.6. The hero is drawn opaque (eye farther than 5). The sight line is blocked while a wall or pillar lies between: for loc 2 the panel 13.1 from the hero is passed at t = 21 (20 blocked ticks, below the 61-tick limit, so there is no recentre and the eye glides in); for loc 3 the pillar 3.3 from the hero lies between the resting point and the hero, so whether the line stays blocked until the 61st tick (recentre: velocity zeroed, eye rebuilt behind or in front of the hero) depends on whether the cell under the eye lists the pillar (Medium-Low). The engine's fade-in begins about 6 passes after the load, so the first visible pictures show the eye at t of about 5 to 9 (loc 2: 69.6 down to 33.7 from the hero): a fast glide in over about 25 ticks (Medium).
Test vectors for the order: (1) a script placement in tick t, then a camera update in tick t+1 with eye E1: the picture of t uses the view built before the placement; the picture of t+1 uses E1 after follow tick 1, not the placed eye. (2) Door contact teleport into a resident world in tick T0: T0 draws the new world with the old view, T1 is the first correct picture. (3) Enter-interior job with a clean loader: placement tick R, fade-in starts on R+4 with first level 120.9; leave-interior job: placement tick L+2 with the fade opaque since L. (4) Camera ground world after a switch in tick R: the exterior until the end of R, the interior after R+1's ground clearance, and the wall pass of R+1 already reads the interior.

**Fields: reset or kept by an entry placement.**
| Field | At a placement |
| --- | --- |
| Eye (0xE0), previous eye (0xF0), position word (0x30) | set to the clipped eye |
| Eye motion direction (0x100) | cleared to (0, 0, 0, 1) |
| Current and target polar (0x130 to 0x14C), manual elevation (0xC4), elevation target (0x154), spin (0x150), side-swing counter (0x1CC), reseat flag (0x198) | reset from the new eye and focus (Camera reset) |
| Manual flag (0xC8), R3 recentre latch (0x1D0), camera ground entry (0x1D4) | cleared by the attach only (default setup, hero switch, start of a game); not by the pose setter alone |
| Camera ground world (0x1D8) and ground height (0x1DC) | kept: stale until the first follow tick's ground clearance writes them |
| Spring velocity (0x110) | kept. It is zeroed only when the camera record is first built. A camera that had settled has a velocity near zero; a camera that was moving (a teleport during a run) carries its velocity into the new world |
| Contact normals (0x280 and following) and their count (0x500), stuck counter (0x510), failure flags (0x504, 0x508), restore latch (0x50C), line-of-sight words (0x514 to 0x51C) | kept. The contact-block reset that clears the flags and counters runs only when the camera is built at start-up, never at an entry (and it leaves the contact list alone anyway). Contact entries age only while the hero moves or the hold word is set (Walls and the stuck timers), so an entry made in the old world can still be present in the new one |
| Hold counter, side offset and target, side lean, biases, turn leans, side-swing increment | kept |
| Profile index | kept. A follow tick reselects the profile from the hero's ground entry only when the hero has one; an entry clears that entry, so the first ticks use the old world's profile until the hero's ground probe finds a patch |
| Focal length | 512 every tick |
| Hero fade level | recomputed on every follow tick from the eye-to-focus distance (Fade ramp) |

**First-frame eye for the angle form.** SetToNormalCamera(distance, angle, release): with A the hero's forward row and B its first row (world matrix rows at +0x2B0 and +0x290) and base the hero's feet position at the moment of the call, the wanted eye is W = base + distance x unit(A cos(angle) + B sin(angle)). A and B are orthonormal, so the unit vector is already of length 1. The eye is not taken from the old world, not blended and not interpolated: the setter snaps it. Steps: (1) F = base + (0, 3.5, 0) (hero height 3.5, 0.25 of it in hero state 21, plus the small bias); (2) E1 = (W.x, F.y, W.z); (3) the ground height g at (W.x, W.z), probed from F.y + 5: if g is above F.y + 5, E1 = F + 0.5 (E1 - F) horizontally (y stays F.y); (4) the occlusion clip moves E1 toward F as below; (5) reset. Angle 0 is in front of the hero, pi behind it, pi/2 on its first-row side. The result has elevation 0 about F, whatever the profile elevation is.

**Occlusion clip** (the pose setter, the recentre commit and the last step of every attach; High for the face test, the group prefilter, the order, the list A kinds, the per-kind shapes, the box face set and the two timers' separation from it; from the game code, read word by word 2026-11-08, not run in the original). The clip routine returns nothing and works on the camera position word (0x30). It runs only when the camera's ground-world word is nonzero, on the camera position word (the eye the setter has just written), and its tests run in this order, each seeing the eye as moved by the previous one. F is the focus point (the focus quad at 0x120) and r_h = 1.175 is the hero's body radius: a value read at run time from the focus object's motion record (the same for both heroes), not a constant of the routine; the camera's own 1.25 is not used here.
1. The world's wall-face groups (the type-10 ladder and climb-panel table of SPEC-0006: a centre C, a squared radius Q and a list of faces). A group is tested only when |C - eye|^2 / 2 < Q, with |C - eye| the full three-dimensional distance from the camera position word. No direction is used; the test is a plain distance test that admits a group while the eye is within sqrt(2 Q) = 1.414 sqrt(Q) of its centre. Every face of an admitted group goes through the face test below, whatever its type (-3 ladder, -2 panel, up-facing strips included). High.
2. The pool of plate records of the world (count at world +0x2D40, entries from +0x2D50, 0x160 bytes each): for each entry whose used word (+4) is zero, the kind 3 cylinder test (below) on the entry's primitive (entry +0xC0), then advance to the next entry. The pool cursor advances only after a tested entry: an entry whose used word is nonzero is never stepped over (the loop count still drops), so that entry and every entry after it are never visited. The pool has no shipped additions, so this is a static reading (High for the code).
3. The grid cell under the camera position (x and z of the position word, looked up once): (a) its frame records except type 6, by the face test below, with no entry gate (the per-tick pass skips an entry whose gate word fails the world's collision word; this clip does not read the gate, so a gated frame record clips as well; High); (b) its list A primitives by kind (the frame records come first, then list A). Only kinds 1, 3, 4 and 7 do anything: kind 2 is skipped and every other kind does nothing. No mask or class bit is read by the clip routine itself; the gates are inside the per-kind tests, which read the mask against the collision word of the world in the camera's ground-world word (0x1D8), a zero mask passing: kind 1 by the three-ray sphere test, kind 3 by the capped cylinder test (class bits as in Walls and the stuck timers), kinds 4 and 7 with a radius value (third extent, float at +0x2C) at or below 0 by the rectangle clip (class bit 0 or 5 required), kinds 4 and 7 with a positive radius value by the box ray test (class bit 3, else class bit 0, required; the clip passes skip-gates 0).
4. The world's near-tree list (count at world +0x2CD0, patches at +0x2CD4; foliage patches, not characters): for each patch and each plant (count at patch +0x94, 0x70-byte records from patch +0x98, base position at plant +0x30) the trunk test (below) with the patch's radius (the w lane, +0xFC, of the patch's quad at +0xF0) and height (patch +0xE4), both read once per patch. (The earlier reading "characters listed by the world's regions" is withdrawn.)

**Face test (frame records and wall-group faces; exact).** A face has a unit normal n with plane offset o (signed distance of p = n . p + o), a run plane U (unit horizontal run direction, value 0 at the face's start), a length L, a top plane M with depth D (SPEC-0018, Frame record as an edge; type-10 faces have the same fields with M the up axis, v = D at the top edge). Let E = the eye, F = the focus, ell = |E - F| (a distance in world units).
- Shifted end: E' = E - r_h n. The end is moved against the normal, toward the back of the face, whichever side the eye is on. dF = n . F + o, dE = n . E' + o. When dF = dE there is no hit.
- t = dF / (dF - dE), the fraction of the shifted segment F to E' at which it crosses the plane (a pure fraction, not a distance; stored even when the window then fails). A hit needs -0.02 <= t <= 1.02 (both ends inclusive). The test is two-sided: the sign of dF is not read. For a focus in front of the face (dF > 0) a hit needs dE <= 0.0196 dF, that is an eye in front of the face (dP = n . E + o) must be within r_h + 0.0196 dF of the plane; an eye farther out than that is not touched, and an eye behind the face is always crossed.
- X = F + t (E' - F). The crossing must lie in the face rectangle: u = U . (X, 1) in [-0.25, L + 0.25] and v = M . (X, 1) in [-0.25, D - 0.25]. The run extent is widened by 0.25 at both ends. The vertical extent is widened by 0.25 below the stored depth and cut by 0.25 at the top: with the top-edge height H_top at X (SPEC-0018) the crossing's height y must satisfy H_top - D - 0.25 <= y <= H_top - 0.25, with H_top the height of the top plane itself at X. (v = D - (H_top - y) for a vertical M, so v <= D - 0.25 is y <= H_top - 0.25 and v >= -0.25 is y >= H_top - D - 0.25.) A segment that crosses the top 0.25 of a face, or passes above it, misses.
- On a hit: if ell - t < 1.2 r_h = 1.41, the eye is put on F itself (all four lanes copied from F, so the eye's w is F's w). Otherwise the eye becomes F + t (E - F) with w = 1, that is the fraction t of the shifted segment applied to the unshifted one. ell is a distance and t a fraction, mixed as coded, so the snap threshold is ell < 1.41 + t, between 1.39 and 2.43 for t in [-0.02, 1.02]. Closed form for an eye in front (dP = n . E + o) and a focus in front (dF > 0): the new eye lies dF r_h / (dF - dP + r_h) in front of the plane (less than r_h when dP < r_h, so the clipped eye can sit closer to the face than the 1.25 margin of the per-tick pass).
- The test is repeated for every face in list order, each on the eye left by the previous hit; after a snap to F the later faces see ell = 0 and snap again, which changes nothing.
The per-tick pass uses a different height window (Camera plane test). Two different rectangle tests exist and are not interchangeable: this face test (plane window -0.02 to 1.02, run window widened 0.25, vertical window -0.25 to D - 0.25) serves the frame records and wall-group faces of the clip, the line-of-sight sweep and the world segment query; the exact rectangle test (fraction window [0, 1] and in-plane windows [0, extent], no slack at all) serves the flat-rectangle clip and the five box faces of the box ray test (High; both read from the code 2026-11-08, see SPEC-0018, Segment versus a rectangle).

**Round and cylinder tests (kind 1, kind 3, plate entries, trunks; High; read from the code 2026-11-08).** All of them use the line from S to the eye, with S = F lowered by 1.0 along the up vector (the flat rectangle lowers it by |row 2 vertical| instead). Let unit = (eye - S) / |eye - S| and len = |eye - S| (so len differs from |eye - F|). The hit window is strictly 0 < t < len, with t a distance along unit. On a hit the eye becomes S + unit x (t - 0.05) (w = 1) and the routine reports that the eye moved; a segment that starts inside the shape has t0 <= 0 and uses the exit point, so the eye then sits 0.05 inside the far surface. Hit choice for the cylinder and trunk tests: with one parameter use it if in (0, len); with two use the first (t0) if in (0, len), else the second (t1) if in (0, len); t0 is preferred, not the minimum (for a sloped line t0 is the entry; for a line parallel to the axis t0 is the top cap). Per shape:
- Kind 1 sphere (gates: mask, class bit 0 or 1 set, class bit 2 clear; no distance gate): a true three-dimensional sphere (centre = primitive +0x30 xyz, radius = +0x3C), not a horizontal circle. Three parallel rays of the same direction and the same maximum length len are tried in order and the first hit wins: the centre ray (from S), then the ray shifted by +side, then by -side, with side = normalise(unit x up) x 1.5 (horizontal, perpendicular to the line: (-unit.z, 0, unit.x) normalised, times 1.5; a fat line 3.0 wide in all). Each ray uses the nearest root in (0, len), else the far root (with an end-point-inside check). On a hit the eye is placed on the CENTRE ray: S + unit (t - 0.05), with t of whichever ray hit.
- Kind 3 cylinder and plate-pool entries (gates: mask; class bit 0 or 4 set, bit 0 tested first; then class bit 5 must be clear; then a horizontal gate: the primitive's +0x30 x and z against the eye must be at squared distance below 2500.0, else nothing): a capped finite cylinder along the primitive's OWN axis (the quad at +0x40, not forced to the up vector), base = +0x30, length = +0x24, radius = the w lane of the +0x30 quad (+0x3C; the +0x28 and +0x2C copies are not read); centre = base + axis x length / 2. Height is not ignored: for a vertical axis it is a vertical cylinder from the base up by the length, with end caps.
- Tree trunk (one plant; no mask gate, no class gate): a vertical capped cylinder from the plant's base position up by the patch height with the patch radius (centre = base + up x height / 2). The plant is skipped when the horizontal squared distance between its base and the eye is not below 2500.0 (skipped at exactly 50 and beyond; measured base to eye, not to the focus). Because the segment is three-dimensional and the cylinder is capped, a line passing over the top is clear.
Test vector (trunk): eye (-20, 45, 0), focus (20, 45, 0), plant at base y 40, patch radius 4.95, height 25: S = (20, 44, 0), the line crosses the cylinder (y 40 to 65) and is blocked; with the eye 55 away horizontally the plant is skipped. In the clip the eye would be set at S + unit (t - 0.05).

**Rectangle clip (door-like flat rectangles; High for the geometry, Medium-High for the nudge being zero).** A kind 4 or 7 primitive with a third extent at or below 0 is a flat rectangle: row 0 of its axis rows is the plane normal n_r and rows 1 (at +0x50, extent at +0x28) and 2 (at +0x60, extent at +0x24) are the two in-plane unit axes, with the extents on them (SPEC-0018, Box convention). Gate: mask word then class bit 0 or 5 (no rejecting bit, no distance gate); all 169 door rectangles on disc carry class bit 1 only, so they never clip (and never push or block, see Door objects). For a rectangle that passes: S = F lowered by the absolute value of the vertical component of axis row 2, along the up vector; E = eye - 0.05 n_r. The segment S to E must cross the plane at a fraction t in [0, 1] (exactly: t below 0 or above 1 fails, 0 and 1 pass; no slack; a zero denominator is no hit and writes nothing), at in-plane coordinates in [0, extent] on both axes (u = row 1 . (X, 1) in [0, extent 1], v = row 2 . (X, 1) in [0, extent 2], the end values inside; no slack and no 0.25 anywhere). The test is two-sided (the sign of the plane distance is never read). On a hit the eye becomes S + t (eye - S) (t applied to the UNshifted eye, no 0.05 pull-back) plus 0.45 (b . a) a, with a the in-plane row at +0x50 (row 1; row 0 is the plane normal, so this is not the "second" in-plane row) and b a camera vector word. That word is set to (0, 0, 0, 1) when the camera is built and no code writes it afterwards, so b . a (xyz only) is 0 and the nudge never moves anything in the shipped game (Medium-High, by the absence of a writer). The 0.45 factor is therefore irrelevant; a rewrite may omit the nudge.

**Box ray test (kinds 4 and 7 with a positive third extent; High, including the face set).** Gate: when the skip-gates argument is 0 (it is 0 in both callers): mask word, then class bit 3, else class bit 0 (neither: nothing); a nonzero skip-gates argument skips both gates. S = F lowered by 1.0 (not the flat rectangle's drop). A prefilter tests the line S to eye against a three-dimensional ball (x, y and z all count; not a horizontal circle) around the box's reference point with the radius 20 x the largest of its three extents (almost always passed); no hit returns 0. Then five of the box's six faces are each tested with the plain rectangle crossing (the rectangle test of the Rectangle clip: fraction in [0, 1], in-plane coordinates in [0, extent], no slack, two-sided) for the segment S to the unshifted eye; the face tested first that hits is the initial best, and each later face replaces it only with a smaller fraction; the smallest fraction among the hits is used: the eye becomes S + t_min (eye - S) (plus the zero nudge above, using the best face's in-plane axis), w = 1. No margin and no 0.05 pull-back: the eye ends exactly on the first face the segment enters. A segment lying wholly inside the box crosses no face and leaves the eye where it is. The tested faces are, with row 0 the axis with extent +0x2C, row 1 the axis with extent +0x28 and row 2 the vertical axis with extent +0x24 and reference point o (the low corner): the +row 0 face, the -row 0 face, the +row 1 face, the -row 1 face and the -row 2 (bottom) face. The one face NOT tested is the +row 2 face: the TOP face (the plane through o + extent 2 x row 2). This matches SPEC-0018 (Sweep of a moving body against a box: the four sides and the bottom, never the top). Resolved (High for the face identity, from the five face builders' normals and origins).

**Test vectors for the clip.** Face: normal (0, 0, 1), o = 0 (plane z = 0), run along +x from x = 0, L = 40, depth D = 20 with top height 20 (so the crossing must have y between -0.25 and 19.75), focus F = (10, 5, 8), r_h = 1.175.
| Case | Eye | Computation | New eye |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| eye in front, inside the margin | (10, 5, 0.5) | E' z = -0.675, t = 8 / 8.675 = 0.92219, ell = 7.5, ell - t = 6.58 | (10, 5, 1.08357) |
| eye behind the face | (10, 5, -3) | E' z = -4.175, t = 8 / 12.175 = 0.65708, ell = 11 | (10, 5, 0.77207): pulled through to the focus side |
| eye exactly on the face | (10, 5, 0) | t = 8 / 9.175 = 0.87193, ell = 8 | (10, 5, 1.02452) |
| eye in front, outside the margin | (10, 5, 2.0) | t = 8 / 7.175 = 1.11498, above 1.02 | unchanged |
| short segment (snap) | F = (10, 5, 1.2), eye (10, 5, 0.3) | E' z = -0.875, t = 1.2 / 2.075 = 0.57831, ell = 0.9, ell - t = 0.3217 < 1.41 | (10, 5, 1.2) = F |
| short, just above the threshold | F = (10, 5, 3.5), eye (10, 5, 1.0) | t = 3.5 / 3.675 = 0.95238, ell = 2.5, ell - t = 1.5476 | (10, 5, 1.11905) |
| run window | F = (50, 5, 8), eye (50, 5, -3) | crossing at x = 50 > 40.25 | unchanged |
| top cut (true top 20, D = 120): F y = 19.6 and 19.9, both with eye y the same | eye (10, y, -3) | v = y + (D - top) = 119.6 or 119.9 against the limit 119.75 | y = 19.6 hits and gives (10, 19.6, 0.77207); y = 19.9 misses |
Rectangle (plane z = 0, normal (0, 0, 1), axis row 2 the vertical unit axis, extents large enough, class bit 0 set), F = (10, 5, 8), eye (10, 5, -3): S = (10, 4, 8), E = (10, 5, -3.05), t = 8 / 11.05 = 0.72398, eye = S + t (eye - S) = (10, 4.72398, 0.03620). With only class bit 1 (a door rectangle) the eye stays at (10, 5, -3).
Box (x 0 to 4, z 0 to 4, y 0 to 6, the segment enters through a tested face), F = (2, 5, 8), eye (2, 4.5, -3): S = (2, 4, 8), the segment enters at z = 4: t = 4 / 11 = 0.36364, eye = (2, 4.18182, 4.0).
Wall group: a group with centre (0, 0, 0) and squared radius 100 is tested for an eye at distance 14.0 (196 / 2 = 98 < 100) and skipped at distance 14.2 (201.64 / 2 = 100.82).
Doorway (two faces in the plane z = 0: A run x 0 to 10, B run x 14 to 24, a gap of 4; same depth and top as above; the door rectangle across the gap has class bit 1 only): F = (12, 5, 8), eye (12, 5, -3): the crossing x = 12 is outside both faces widened by 0.25 (A ends at 10.25, B starts at 13.75), so nothing is clipped and the eye stays behind the wall line, outside the doorway. F = (12, 5, 8), eye (9, 5, -3): crossing x = 10.03 is inside face A's widened run, so the eye becomes (10.02875, 5, 0.77207).

**Frames 1 to N after the entry.** Each camera tick runs the normal follow with the first-tick values: the profile is the one in force (see the field table); the target distance is the profile distance x (1 - 0.65 sin^2(e)) with e the stored elevation lane (the reset elevation on ticks 1 and 2, 0 right after a setter placement, so the full distance 10 for profile 0 on those two ticks; from tick 3 it is the elevation of the eye as it stood at the start of the previous tick, see Target distance, one-tick lag); the elevation target starts at the reset elevation and rises by 0.01 per tick toward the profile elevation (0.2) while the hero is idle, the stick centred and the manual flag clear; the yaw is the eye's own, so the eye is pulled along its own side of the hero, not swung to the hero's back. The spring (k = 0.085, c = 0.583095) closes the gap as in Follow lag (0.915, 0.80179 and 0.68644 of the gap after ticks 1 to 3, 0.486 after 5, 0.193 after 10, 0.030 after 20); there is no extra blend or glide. The eye is then tested every tick: ground clearance first (eye at least 2.0 above the ground under it), then the wall pass (swept eye sphere of radius 1.25 against the object records, the cell's frame records except type 6, its primitives and the near-tree trunks), then the stuck timer (more than 3 failing ticks) and the line-of-sight timer (more than 60 blocked ticks), each running the recentre pass of Recentre pass and R3. So on tick 1 the original does test the eye against the ground and the walls, with the world under the eye: the follow's ground clearance probes the current world first, then the other resident world, and stores the world that supplied the entry (the current world when both miss) in the ground-world word before the wall pass reads it.
**Eye frozen against a remembered wall.** The spring velocity is added to the eye each tick, except that while the hero is idle (body speed at most 0.01, not in state 28) and the stuck counter is 0, the move is skipped when the velocity has a non-positive dot product with any contact normal in the list. A wall contact on the first tick therefore pins the eye in front of the wall for as long as the hero stands still, while the velocity keeps growing toward the target. The contact list is not cleared by an entry, so a normal left from the old world can pin the eye in the new world in the same way (High for the rule; Medium for how often it happens).
**Eye inside a wall.** (a) At the setter, the clip pulls the eye to the first face crossed on the way to the focus, provided that face belongs to the camera's ground world. (b) On the first tick, the wall pass pushes the eye out of a face, sphere, cylinder or box to the distance 1.25 on the open side along the contact normal (depth = 1.25 - signed distance), and adds the normal to the contact list. (c) If a contact cannot be resolved the failure flag is set; the fourth consecutive failing tick runs the recentre pass. (d) If the eye is not pushed and the segment from the eye to the focus stays blocked, the 61st blocked tick (more than 60, that is 1.0 s) runs the recentre pass: the camera is rebuilt behind the hero at the target distance and profile elevation; if that line is blocked, in front of the hero at the target distance; if that is blocked too, the behind candidate is shrunk by 0.25 per blocked test while its radius is above 1.0 (the last radius lies in (0.75, 1.0]); then the occlusion clip and camera reset. For (b) when the eye starts wholly behind a one-sided face (it and its tick-start position both behind the plane) the wall pass reports no contact and leaves it there (Eye starting behind a panel or inside a volume; High).
**Camera inside geometry handling.** The only explicit handling is the hero fade: when the eye is closer than 1.7 to the focus the hero's fade level is 0 at once, from 1.7 to 5.0 it ramps (Fade ramp). There is no near-plane shift (the near plane is 1.0 for the whole game), no hiding or alpha fade of wall or door objects between the eye and the hero, and no separate camera-inside-geometry test; the near-plane bias scalar of the atmosphere zones changes the depth mapping only (SPEC-0028) (Medium-High).

**Test vector: hero 2 units in front of a wall, facing away, eye 10 behind.** Profile 0 (distance 10, elevation 0.2, k 0.085, c 0.583095); hero feet (0, 0, 0), facing (0, 0, 1), so A = (0, 0, 1) and B = (1, 0, 0); height 3.5, so F = (0, 3.5, 0); one wall face on the plane z = -2 with its normal (0, 0, 1) pointing at the hero, wide enough to cover the eye; floor at y = 0; hero idle, sticks centred, camera velocity 0 and no stored contacts; the wall belongs to the camera's ground world; the call is SetToNormalCamera(10.0, 3.14159265, true). Only y and z are listed (x stays 0).
| Frame | What happens | Eye (y, z) | Eye distance to F | Velocity (y, z) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 (entry) | W = (0, 0, -10); set to the focus height: (3.5, -10); the ground is fine; clip: shifted end z = -11.175, crossing fraction t = 2 / 11.175 = 0.178971, 10 - t is above 1.41, so the eye is F + t (E - F); reset: polar (1.78971, -pi/2, 0); hero state 1 | (3.5, -1.78971) | 1.78971 | (0, 0) |
| 1 | target distance 10 (elevation 0); elevation target 0.01; target point (3.6, -9.9995); velocity = -0.085 (E - T) = (0.0085, -0.69783); no stored contact, so the eye moves to z = -2.48754; the wall pass finds it 0.48754 behind the plane and pushes it to 1.25 in front, z = -0.75; contact normal (0, 0, 1) stored; clear line to the focus, no failure; the hero fade level drops to 0 (distance below 1.7) | (3.5085, -0.75) | 0.75005 | (0.0085, -0.69783) |
| 2 | target distance 10.0 (the stored elevation lane is still the reset 0: it holds the polar of the eye as it stood at the start of tick 1; see Target distance, one-tick lag); elevation target 0.02; target point (3.69999, -9.99800); velocity (0.01982, -1.07701); hero idle, stuck counter 0, velocity . normal = -1.07701 is not positive: the eye does not move | (3.5085, -0.75) | 0.75005 | (0.01982, -1.07701) |
| 3 | target distance 9.99917 (stored elevation 0.01133, the eye at the start of tick 2); elevation target 0.03; the eye is frozen | (3.5085, -0.75) | 0.75005 | (0.03303, -1.23481) |
| 4 | elevation target 0.04; frozen | (3.5085, -0.75) | 0.75005 | (0.04704, -1.30030) |
| 5 | elevation target 0.05; frozen | (3.5085, -0.75) | 0.75005 | (0.06137, -1.32722) |
The 9.99917 distance first appears at tick 3 (an earlier draft of this table put it at tick 2 and listed the velocity z as -1.07694, -1.23478, -1.30028 and -1.32721; the values here are recomputed with the lagged elevation and agree with the model check of the census to 7e-5; the velocity y values are unchanged to the digits shown).
Later ticks: the velocity z settles near -1.343 and its y keeps rising by about 0.014 per tick while the elevation target climbs to 0.2; the eye stays at (3.5085, -0.75) while the hero stands still. No timer runs (the line to the focus is clear and no contact fails), so there is no recentre. On the first tick on which the hero moves the stored velocity acts: the eye is shoved into the wall and pushed back to 1.25 in front each tick, sliding along it, and the stored normal ages out over 40 moving ticks. The hero is drawn with fade level 0 while the eye is within 1.7 of F. Medium for the pinned end state: it rests on the rule that the wall pass reports and resolves the contact on tick 1 and that the contact list holds the normal.
Variant (the wall is not in the camera's ground world at the entry, for example an interior door whose wall belongs to the new world): frame 0 has no clip, the eye is (3.5, -10), behind the wall; on tick 1 the velocity is almost 0 (the gap in z is 0.0005), so the eye stays behind the wall: the wall pass ignores an eye wholly behind a one-sided face (High); if it is not pushed, the line to the focus is blocked on every tick, and on the 61st tick the recentre pass runs: the behind candidate (3.5 + 10 sin 0.2, -10 cos 0.2) = (5.4867, -9.8007) is blocked by the wall, the in-front candidate about 10 units on the hero's facing side is clear and is used, so the camera ends in front of the hero looking back at him.
Test vector (no wall in range): the same call: frame 0 eye (3.5, -10), polar (10, -pi/2, 0), velocity 0. The spring then lifts the eye toward elevation 0.2 and holds distance near 10 on the same side; the distance and elevation gaps shrink by the factors 0.915, 0.80179 and 0.68644 after ticks 1 to 3.

### Arrival census by pack (door, teleport and world-entry arrivals) (Confidence: High for the calls, locators, positions, facings and call order; Medium-High for the measured distances, which use static data only; Medium for what the groups mean on screen. From the game code and disc data; not run in the original)

**What was measured.** Every arrival that places the hero at a locator (entry record) of the receiving pack: the activate-world and reactivate routines (one row per value arm), the start routines of the interior packs (hero at entry record 0), and the engine placements of loads and door landings (Camera on world entry). For each arrival the hero stands at the record's position and faces the record's facing; the feet are the ground probe started 4.0 above the record, and the focus is the feet plus 3.5. A wall "behind" or "ahead" is the nearest one on the horizontal ray through the focus along the reverse facing or the facing, out to 16 units. A wall is a level-mesh panel (a frame record of the pack's model sets, skipping type 6 and gated entries; the panel hangs from its stored top edge down its stored depth) or a solid collision volume (a box or rectangle that passes the camera's class and mask gates). Pillars and balls (cylinder and sphere volumes) are counted apart from walls. Door rectangles (kind 7, header flags 0x2) are reported apart, because the camera ignores them: they carry only class bit 1, and every camera route (the per-tick eye pass, the line-of-sight sweep, the occlusion clip) accepts a flat rectangle only with class bit 0 or 5. All 169 doors on disc carry only class bit 1, so no door rectangle ever blocks or pushes the camera. Moving objects, the frame records of placed objects, tree trunks and gated walls were not measured in this census; Objects and the camera (Collision and occlusion) measures them.

**Findings.**
1. **Scripted door arrivals put the eye in front of the hero.** All 174 arrival rows of the level modules (138 value arms in 52 packs, 36 start routines in 36 packs; 86 packs in all) call SetToNormalCamera(distance, angle, true) after (or, in two packs, before) the MoveHero of the arm. The angle is 0.0 in 170 rows and about pi in 4 (pack 985 locator 9, distance 3.0; pack 1096 locator 10, 5.0; pack 1174 locator 0, 6.0; pack 1381 locator 0, 10.0). Angle 0 puts the eye on the facing side of the hero (Return to normal with an angle), and the locator facings on disc point away from the door the hero came through. Distances: 5.0 (87 rows), 10.0 (41), 15.0 (38), 20.0 (3), 6.0, 3.0 and 2.0 (one each; 2.0 is pack 1098 value 100).
2. **A wall or door lies behind the hero within the camera distance in most arrivals.** In 115 of the 170 angle-0 rows a wall (100 rows, nearest 4.0 away at the median) or only a door rectangle (15 rows) lies behind the hero within the call distance plus the eye margin 1.25; in 128 of the 174 rows one lies within the distance of the engine's own restart placement (10 behind). The front eye is clear of any wall in 109 of those 115 rows. A camera placed behind the hero at the same distance would start behind a wall or past a door in all 115. The six exceptions are listed in group 4.
3. **The four rows with angle about pi have clear ground behind them.** Packs 985, 1096 and 1381 have no wall within 16 behind; pack 1174 has its nearest wall 11.98 behind against the distance 6.0.
4. **Departure callbacks do not touch the camera.** The teleport and door callbacks only post the message value, start the load and wait; the camera is placed by the engine's entry placement (Camera on world entry) and then re-placed by the arrival routine of the receiving pack (see the tables). Two departure routines play a scene first: the door of pack 934 (a track play and a 1.5 s wait, then ReturnToNormalCamera, before the load) and the mine exit of pack 864 (two track plays, a walk and a fade, then ReturnToNormalCamera, before the load).
5. **Order quirks.** Pack 1056 calls SetToNormalCamera before MoveHero on all three arrivals (values 30, 40, 50 to locators 4, 2, 3). The call uses the hero's position at that moment, which is entry record 0 of the load, so the eye is 10 in front of entry record 0 (51.93, 26.5, 392.5) while the hero is then moved 61.6, 132.6 and 207.5 units away; a solid obstacle lies between the eye and the hero for locators 2 and 3 (a wall panel for locator 2, a pillar volume for locator 3). The follow camera then pulls the eye in, and the line-of-sight timer (more than 60 blocked ticks) ends in the recentre pass. Pack 1174 also calls before the move, but moves to the same entry record. Pack 1300 value 60 (return from the shop interior) and pack 822 value 1 call ReturnToNormalCamera(-1, true) instead, which places nothing in mode 0, so the eye stays where the engine's placement left it. Pack 1139 makes the same call twice.

**Groups: how the original avoids a camera clipping into a wall or door at an arrival.**
1. **Angle 0 with a locator facing away from the door (eye in front): 109 rows in 52 packs** (722, 748, 749, 750, 758, 776, 777, 786 to 789, 880, 893, 906, 909, 951, 956, 984, 991, 1005, 1008, 1011, 1017, 1020, 1023, 1056, 1098, 1139, 1143, 1152, 1159, 1163, 1172, 1173, 1257, 1300, 1324, 1327, 1335, 1350, 1353, 1358, 1363, 1371, 1397, 1398, 1405, 1407, 1409, 1414, 1421 and 1425). There is a wall or door behind the hero within the distance, and the eye is clear in front. This is the dominant mechanism; the nearest wall behind is 1.2 to 12.6 away (median 4.0, 94 rows) and a door rectangle lies 0.0 to 12.0 behind (median 3.6, 93 rows).
2. **Short distance: only pack 1174** (6.0 behind, nearest wall 11.98 behind). Distance 5.0 also leaves margin where 10 would not: 24 of the 87 rows with distance 5.0 have their nearest wall 6.3 to 11.3 behind, within reach of the 10-unit restart placement but clear of the 5.0 eye.
3. **No hazard behind, so no avoidance is needed: 55 rows** (35 packs, for example 732, 735, 745, 752, 753, 783, 855 to 859, 985, 986, 1096, 1131, 1133, 1136, 1140, 1148, 1150, 1156, 1170, 1250, 1381 and 1429).
4. **Placed eye itself blocked, left to the camera's own collision: 9 rows** (1014 value 40, wall inside the margin of the eye 4.66 ahead; 1247, 1248 and 1249, panel between hero and the 15.0 eye at 12.9, 10.2 and 9.2; 1411 value 10, box wall 6.85 ahead of the 10.0 eye; start routines of 1414, 1417, 1419 and 1423 with a wall panel 1.3, 11.8, 15.7 and 15.3 ahead of the 15.0 eye). The occlusion clip of the pose setter pulls such an eye in toward the focus when the wall belongs to the camera's ground-world word; otherwise the eye passes the per-tick push (1.25) and the line-of-sight timer (61 blocked ticks, then the recentre pass; Camera on world entry, Eye inside a wall). The tick-by-tick model of Hazard arrivals: expected camera (below) finds only the start routine of 1414 to be a lasting hazard (eye 0.05 from the hero, hero fully faded) and does not confirm the 1411 box wall (that box starts 29.7 above the feet).
5. **Engine placements without a following script call (no avoidance at placement time).** The restart placement (entry record 0 of a replacement load or plain load, eye 10 behind) leaves the eye behind a wall in 45 of the 179 packs that have entry records and within the push radius of one in 15 more; the placement 5 behind (interior jobs and door landings) leaves it behind a wall in 14 and within the radius in 18. In packs with an arrival routine the restart placement is what a plain load without a message value (value -1) shows, because the routine then makes no call (the "Plain load" column below). The door-rule landings (the 45 unscripted doors; 167 doors have a teleport plane in all packs) put the hero 5 in front of the door plane facing the plane normal, so the eye (landing minus 5 times the facing) lies exactly on the door plane: within 1.25 of a wall in 107 of the 167 planes, behind a wall in 7 (pack 722 planes 0 and 3, 947 plane 0, 951 plane 0, 1008 plane 0, 1023 plane 0, 1257 plane 0) and clear in 53. With the facing reversed (the hero walks along the plane normal into the door and arrives facing the door) the eye is clear in 163 of 167. These rely on the occlusion clip, the per-tick push and the two timers only.

**Fades and scripted cameras around the arrival.** Engine: the enter-interior job fades out 0.3 s and fades in 0.3 s after five idle loader passes; the leave-interior job fades out at once and fades in 0.3 s after five idle passes; a script replacement load fades out 6 frames when the exterior is loaded and has no engine fade-in; a door landing with the fade flag requests a cancelling fade pair. The level modules add fades or scripted cameras at an arrival only where the last column of the tables says so (pack 1300's first-arrival scene, Act 2 table below, and the cutscenes of the Act 2 section above). No arrival routine hides, shows or animates a door model; the door rectangles are invisible trigger planes and the visible door is level mesh or a placed object that no arrival routine touches (Medium: object set-up outside the arrival routines was not measured).

**Test vectors (arrival eye; the pull-in setter then sets the eye to the focus height, F.y = feet + 3.5).**
- Pack 1300 value 20: the hero is moved to locator 7, (-130.71, ground 1538.65, -128.88), facing (0.849, 0.529). SetToNormalCamera(15.0, 0.0, true) wants the eye at feet + 15 x facing = (-117.98, 1542.15, -120.95), in front of the hero and clear. A camera 15 behind would be (-143.45, 1542.15, -136.82): the wall panel behind the hero is 6.19 away (its top 26.4 above the feet) and the door-1 rectangle 6.01 away, so that eye would lie 8.8 beyond the wall.
- Pack 1327 value 30: locator 28, (-71.16, ground 8.0, 121.55), facing (0.493, -0.870). SetToNormalCamera(10.0, 0.0, true) wants (-66.23, 11.5, 112.85), clear. Behind the hero lie the door-0 rectangle at 2.31 and a solid box volume at 4.04.
- Pack 1056 value 40: the load puts the hero at entry record 0, (51.93, 22.99, 382.53) facing (0, 0, 1). SetToNormalCamera(10.0, 0.0, true) runs first and wants (51.93, 26.5, 392.5); only then is the hero moved to locator 2, (149.36, 24.15, 482.52): the eye is 132.6 from the hero and a wall panel (top 28.0) lies 13.1 from the hero on the line between them. For value 50 (locator 3, (149.08, 24.15, 575.91)) the eye is 207.5 away with a pillar volume 3.3 from the hero on the line.
- Pack 1098 locator 1 (value 90): the door rectangle is 1.5 behind (z -54.65), a solid box wall 3.65 behind; the call places the eye 5.0 in front (z -61.15): clear (SPEC-0027 test vector above).
- Pack 1299 plain load: entry record 0, (-106.31, 62.30, -51.49) facing (-0.691, -0.723): the restart eye (10 behind) would be at about (-99.4, 65.1, -44.3), 5.5 beyond the wall panel that stands 4.50 behind (top 24.5) with door 0 at 3.83; the arrival routine's SetToNormalCamera(10.0, 0.0, true) puts the eye at (-113.2, 65.1, -58.7) instead.

**Hazard arrivals: expected camera (from a code model of the follow camera; not a run of the game).** These vectors say what the original camera should do on screen over the first 120 ticks for the arrivals the census marked as hazards: the nine rows of group 4 (the placed eye itself blocked), the one row of group 2 (pack 1174), and the engine-only placements of the packs in the table above (restart eye 10 behind and landing eye 5 behind, entry record 0). They were produced by running the rules of this spec tick by tick over the static frame records and volumes of each pack, read from the disc; they are Medium for that reason (no moving objects, no characters, one world; the original was not run).
*Model.* The hero stands idle at the entry record (feet from the ground probe, focus 3.5 above), the sticks are centred, the spring velocity is 0 and no contact is stored. The pose setter places the eye as in Camera on world entry (wanted point, focus height, halving rule, occlusion clip, reset). Each following tick runs: target distance from the elevation of the previous tick's eye before its spring step (one tick of lag; this reproduces the restart settle vector of Follow camera to four decimals), elevation chase 0.01 per tick toward the profile elevation, the spring (k 0.085, damping 0.583095), the idle freeze against stored contact normals, the ground clearance (2.0 above the ground under the eye, sliding along the line to the focus while the hero is idle), the one-sided swept 1.25 push against the frame records of the eye's cell (entry gate word nonzero = off, type 6 skipped; edge height = top minus 0.25) and the box, sphere and cylinder volumes of that cell, the line-of-sight timer (the 61st blocked tick runs the recentre pass: behind, else in front, else shrink), and the fade ramp (alpha = fade level / 128, level 128 at the entry). Positions are world coordinates to 0.01. "Eye beyond a wall" lists the ticks at which the segment from the focus to the eye crosses any solid frame record or box anywhere along its length (not only in the cells the camera itself tests).
*Which world the clip sees.* The occlusion clip of the setter reads the camera's stored ground world. When a follow tick has already run in the new world (a script call that comes at least one frame after the world became current: the start routines and activate callbacks) the clip sees the new world's walls: these are the rows without a note. For the engine placements, which run in the same pass as the world change, the clip sees the old world and has no effect: those rows were run without the clip. For the module rows whose outcome changes without the clip, the second set of lines (marked old ground world) gives the values. Which set applies follows the Tick table of Camera on world entry: after a driver load or an enter-interior job the arrival call runs on the next tick, after a follow pass has written the new ground world, so the rows without a note apply; the old-ground-world lines apply only when the call runs in the same tick as the switch (a hero door-contact teleport, or the leave-interior job). Every start-routine row above (1414, 1417, 1419, 1423) is a load or an interior entry, so its first line applies (Medium-High).
